## Decided

| D118 | **The 11px type floor: the layer that carries every number the site claims is no longer the smallest text on the page** | Built to `docs/plan/type-floor.md`, the plan the owner approved, committed on the branch before any file changed. **The measurement, walked on the render rather than read off the stylesheet:** 128 elements owned visible text under 11px — 71 on the home page, 12 on each of the four project pages, 3 each on `/experience`, `/how-i-build` and `/cv` — identical at 1440 and 390, because there was no responsive relief at all. Smallest HTML text 9.44px. **After: 0, on every route at both widths.** **IT IS THREE CHANGES, NOT ONE, AND THE FIRST ALONE DID NOT WORK.** (1) SIZE: one value, `0.6875rem`, replacing 0.59/0.6/0.62/0.625/0.64/0.66 — six steps that were never a scale, all under the floor. (2) TRACKING: two values, 0.16em for short terms and 0.13em for long tracked-caps lines and width-boxed chips, replacing eight. (3) LEADING: `line-height: 1.2` on seven label rules. **THE PLAN'S HEADROOM FIGURE WAS WRONG, AND FINDING THAT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THIS PACKAGE.** The plan says 164px of content headroom at desktop. Measured with the same device profile `plate-height.spec.js` uses, the real worst case before the change was `/projects/narratwin` `#narratwin` at **988px against a 990px ceiling — two pixels.** The plan measured content extent and forgot the plate's own block padding. Size and tracking alone took that plate to **exactly 990/990**, passing only because the comparison is `raw > max`, on a gate that runs on Linux where D115 measured plate heights differing from darwin by up to 42px. Had it shipped there it would have been a coin flip. **The leading fix is what made it fit, and it is not a trick to pass a gate.** DESIGN.md's `label` token has carried `lineHeight: 1.2` since the system was written and no CSS rule ever set it, so every label inherited the body's 1.6 — a 9.6px label with 60% leading, which is why the register read as both tiny and loose. Implementing the declared value took `#narratwin` to **967 (23px of margin)** and mobile's worst, `/projects/saafsaans` `#saafsaans`, from 14px to 50px. Worst margins now: desktop 23px, mobile 50px. **Review changed the design twice, and both changes came from measurement, not opinion.** A first attempt dropped tracking 0.02em uniformly; a reviewer measured that this produced SEVEN tracking values at one size and that two of them made the label both bigger AND looser — `.private-card .seal` went 2.182px to 2.20px of absolute tracking, the exact failure `DESIGN.md:206` warns about. The two-value scheme replaced it and also buys width where it is scarcest: the tightest container on the site is a caption cell with 4.1px of slack after the size rise, and 0.2em to 0.16em returns 6.2px. A second reviewer proved two claims in the new gate's own header FALSE by execution — `.rec li::marker` was cited as the 9px case and measures 11.424px, and the gate structurally **cannot** see a pseudo-element because `createTreeWalker(SHOW_ELEMENT)` does not visit one. Both corrected, and the pseudo-element blindness is now a stated boundary in the file rather than a claim of coverage it does not have. **`getComputedStyle` on SVG text reports USER UNITS, not rendered pixels** — the private figure's tags read 9 and 9.5 there and render 6.26px at 390, 8.11px at 1024, 10.70px at 1440. They are the smallest text on the site and no rem value reaches them; filed as DEF-63, measured, not fixed. **DESIGN.md amended in the same change**, because leaving the doc saying `0.6rem` is how this drifted: four scale keys holding four sub-floor values collapse to one `label-min`, two of which named components deleted long ago, and the Label register line carries three corrections the CSS had already outgrown — the weight floor was 520 not 560, the tracking range already reached 0.09em, and "swatch names" named nothing. **Both baselines drained in ONE commit** from dispatch run `32773285903` with both inputs set, so the geometry JSON and the 60 PNGs come from the same runner at the same commit. Files chosen by decoding every PNG and comparing PIXELS, never bytes — 34 changed size, 18 moved pixels at the same size, and 8 came back pixel-identical and were LEFT ALONE. Those 8 are the six `nav-*` snapshots and `plate-overview-1440` in both projects, which is the right answer and is the check working: nav links are 0.76rem and were never under the floor. Geometry: 42 legs, 828 keys, 126 changed, leg count unchanged so no plate appeared or vanished. **ROUND-ONE REVIEW FOUND FOUR REAL HOLES IN THE NEW GATE AND ALL FOUR ARE FIXED AND MUTATION-PROVED.** The cross-model reviewer (`codex exec`, GPT family — genuinely different weights, unlike the same-session subagents, and no independence is claimed for those) attacked the gate rather than the CSS, which is where the value was. (1) The SVG exemption was a whole NAMESPACE, so `<svg><text style="font-size:1px">` would have passed forever; it is now pinned to two named strings and the spec ASSERTS that set, so a third fails rather than joining silently. (2) Text behind a closed `<details>` was never measured — `/cv` ships two, and `.cv-gate-k` at 0.5rem stayed green; the walker now opens every disclosure and the count is asserted so the step cannot no-op. (3) `/404` is a real shipped page and was not in the route list at all — the DEF-10 shape again. (4) Rounding to 2dp before comparing granted a 0.005px pass band: `font-size: 10.999px` cleared an 11px floor. **A fifth hole took two attempts and the second one is the interesting record.** The reviewer's `display: contents` mutation — visible text whose owner has no box — survived the first fix, because `checkVisibility()` returns FALSE for `display: contents` and dropped the element before the new Range test ever ran. Measured on the mutation: `checkVisibility` false, element rects 0, **Range rects 119x9**. `checkVisibility` is gone; painting is now decided by `Range.getClientRects()` on the text nodes and `visibility` is read from the computed style, which is inherited and therefore ancestor-aware without a walk. **All six mutations were RUN, not reasoned about**, and both directions are proved: each turns the gate red, and `visibility: hidden` on the same element is proved to STILL be skipped, so the fix did not buy its recall with a false positive. **The reviewer also refuted three of my own claims by reading the CSS**, and DESIGN.md is corrected accordingly: the Label register's weight is 400–700 not 520–640, its size reaches 0.8rem at the skip link not 0.78, and the leading is 1.2 only on rules that own their own line box — four do not set it and each exception is now named with its reason. The scale comment said four sub-floor keys and there were five; `plate-no` at 0.68rem is itself 10.88px. `line-height: 1.2` was added to the hero-practice block and the motion toggle in the same round, which is what the reviewer's inconsistency finding actually pointed at — 11px labels at 1.6 leading sitting beside 11px labels at 1.2. It shrank the exempt hero plate 1030 to 1019 at desktop and 2516 to 2483 at mobile, and moved no gated plate. **REVIEW STOPPED AT ROUND ONE.** No circuit breaker fired: no new class appeared in a second round, and no fix introduced a defect — the `display: contents` fix needed a second attempt but the first attempt was incomplete, not harmful, and the gap was found by running the mutation rather than by trusting the edit. **Residuals written down rather than fixed:** DEF-63 (the SVG labels), DEF-64 (four DESIGN.md blocks still mandating a removed component), and `print.css:60`'s `0.72em` computing to 8.76px on paper, which no gate covers and this package deliberately scoped out | `docs/plan/type-floor.md` · `tests/type-floor.spec.js` · dispatch `32773285903` · DEF-63 · D115 · D117 · D111 |
| D117 | **DEF-59 fixed: a committed baseline can no longer be written by a laptop, and the rule is git's own answer rather than a list of platforms** | `tests/geometry-baseline.linux.json` and the 60 `*-linux.png` snapshots are tracked while every other platform's equivalent is gitignored. On a Mac the documented regeneration command writes a file nobody reads; on Linux the identical command wrote the committed authority, and the run did not even look wrong, because `geometry.spec.js` skips every comparison while `UPDATE_GEOMETRY` is set. **The defect, measured before anything was built:** calling `writeBaseline()` on the committed path took it from 41,333 bytes and 42 legs to 5 bytes containing `{}`, and `git status` reported an ordinary ` M`. **The fix** is `tests/lib/baseline-write-guard.mjs`. It never mentions a platform. It asks `git ls-files` whether the target is tracked and refuses if it is, exempting only a run where `GITHUB_ACTIONS` is set — so a `win32` baseline someone commits next year is protected the day it lands. It also compares DEVICE AND INODE, because git answers about names and a symlink, a hard link or a case-different spelling is a second name for the same bytes. And it returns the path it actually checked, so the caller cannot resolve that name against a different root than the checker did. **Three wiring points:** `writeBaseline()` for the geometry JSON; `playwright.config.js` for the PNGs, which Playwright writes itself — the config is the seam because it runs before any test and can read the whole command line, verified by experiment; and `updateSnapshots: 'none'` in that same config, because Playwright's default is `missing`, which WRITES any snapshot that does not exist yet and fails that one run, leaving the file on disk for the next `git add -A`. That is how 54 laptop-made files landed once already (`d25b0fc`). **`.gitignore` generalized in the same change:** the PNG rule named `darwin`, so a first run on any other platform wrote un-ignored files. It now ignores every platform's PNG and un-ignores the linux one, the same correction the geometry rule already carried. **`GEOMETRY_BASELINE_OUT`** points the write at a gitignored name. It redirects the WRITE only — the comparison always reads the committed authority, which is correct and is the opposite of the `snapshotPathTemplate` trap. A Linux developer therefore has a scratch file to `diff`, not a local loop; an earlier draft claimed a loop and a reviewer proved it wrong. **Proved to bite, watched:** force-tracking the darwin baseline reproduces the Linux condition on a Mac and the documented command fails with `refusing to write`; force-tracking one `-darwin.png` makes `-u` throw at config load. Both reverted, tree verified clean. **The self-test** (`tests/baseline-guard-selftest.mjs`, 28 checks, in `gates.yml`'s cheap job before `gitleaks` for DEF-39's reason) covers four directions: the guard bites; it does not over-fire, including the two runner cells that keep the dispatch open; the REAL git lookup is driven, including its failure branch and a real symlink; and all three wiring points are exercised rather than grepped for. **Review changed the design, and this is the honest record of it.** A first version passed 20 checks and a mutation reviewer found four mutations that left them all green, plus a cross-model reviewer (`codex exec`, different model family) found three live bypasses by running them: `-uall` was a real Playwright spelling the parser missed; the git lookup was cwd-relative, so `cd tests && npx playwright test --config ../playwright.config.js -u` found nothing tracked; and the claim that Playwright's default "writes and passes" was false against the installed 1.62.1 — it writes and fails. All fixed, and the seven mutations are now caught. **The sanctioned path was not trusted to reasoning.** Three dispatch runs: `32763604696` and `32765812363` (geometry) each returned a 42-leg, 41,333-byte artifact byte-identical to the committed file, and `32765768697` (visual) regenerated all 60 PNGs through `--update-snapshots=all` with the guard active — 26 came back byte-identical and 34 re-encoded, which is D111's documented pattern and the reason only pixel-changed files are committed. **THE REVIEW CIRCUIT BREAKER FIRED, and this row exists partly to record that.** Round two produced a NEW CLASS of blocking finding, and it was introduced by a round-one fix: pinning the git lookup to the repo root left the guard checking one root while `writeFileSync` used another, so from `tests/` the redirect could still truncate the committed baseline. Diagnosed as an understanding miss — a path STRING was being treated as identifying a FILE, which is the same mistake as the alias gap — and fixed at the contract (resolve once, return what was checked, compare inodes) rather than patched at the instance. Then STOPPED, per AGENTS.md: no third round. The residuals are written into the guard's own header under "WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO", and the largest of them is that the PNG half has no alias comparison | Dispatch `32763604696` (success, artifact verified). PR run and the DEF-59 row. Reviewers: one cross-model (`codex`), two same-session subagents — context isolation, not model-weight independence, and no independence is claimed for those two |
| D115 | **DEF-54 fixed: the blocking gate on this site's layout is now a number, not a photograph** | Built to `docs/plan/geometry-gate.md`, the plan the owner approved. `tests/geometry.spec.js` asserts, against the committed text baseline `tests/geometry-baseline.linux.json`, every `.plate[id]` box as `[x, width, height, gapAbove]`, the nav's height, and every structural CTA row's child count, child tag sequence, and per-child box — 42 legs (3 widths x 7 routes x 2 Playwright projects), 828 measured elements, 914 readable lines. **The headline proof, run as a true replay rather than simulated:** a `git worktree` at `df551e9` (the commit BEFORE D112) generated a baseline that never knew the copy button, and the current page was measured against it. At 1440px — the exact width where CI run `32707441086` PASSED the pixel gate with a whole new button present — geometry goes RED naming the row: `row.contact/ctas#0.count [4,4] -> [5,5]`, `row.contact/ctas#0.tags "a,a,a,a" -> "a,button,a,a,a"`, plus a `child.4` measured but not in the baseline. The direction matters and was chosen deliberately: D112 ADDED a child, so the honest replay is baseline-without-button against page-with-button, which exercises the extra-key arm of the comparison. The reverse direction would pass even if the compare only iterated baseline keys and could never see an addition. **Acceptance item 9, also run:** deleting the `.contact .ctas` grid rule from `global.css` turned exactly 4 legs red (390 and 768, both projects) and left all 14 legs at 1440 green, which is correct — the rule is `max-width: 900px`. The child COUNT does not change there (5 before, 5 after); it is caught purely by the boxes, largest delta 180px. `global.css` restored and `git diff` confirmed clean of it. **Three design choices that were measured, not assumed.** (1) Plates store the SEAM GAP above them, not an absolute `y`: a 1px height change in the hero would otherwise rewrite the `y` of all nine plates at every width in both projects, and the gap is itself one of the three things the pixel gate's own header said it existed to watch. (2) Row child boxes are relative to their row, and `motion.css`'s resting `translateY(16px)` is neutralized: `getBoundingClientRect()` includes transforms, so an unrevealed plate reads 16px low — sixteen times the slack, varying with scroll history. Measured both ways; with the transform neutralized, row boxes are identical before and after scrolling `#contact` into view. (3) The baseline records the JS-ON world. Measured: plate boxes are IDENTICAL with JavaScript on and off, and every difference is confined to the contact CTA row — the painted count drops 5 to 4, the button's own child value becomes `hidden`, and the four remaining controls lay out differently because the <=900px grid rule still counts five DOM children. **Correction:** this row first said only the count differs. That was wrong, and a cross-model reviewer caught it. **The plan's own premise was REFUTED by its acceptance item 6, which is the single most important thing in this package.** The plan said the baseline could be generated on any machine "because numbers do not vary by OS the way anti-aliasing does", and marked it *must be verified, not assumed*. It was verified, and it is wrong. A baseline generated on ubuntu-latest by CI run `32738865852` was diffed against one generated on darwin at the same commit with no source change between them: of **828 keys, 284 differed and 148 were PAST THE SLACK, the worst by 42px** (`plate.quorum-record` at 390). The first CI run of this branch (`32738854582`) was red with **38 of 42 legs failing**, on an untouched tree. The deltas land on plate HEIGHTS and link WIDTHS, which is the tell: macOS and Linux rasterize the same self-hosted font files to different advance widths, so text wraps at different points, so blocks are different heights. **Geometry does not escape font rendering — it inherits it through the wrap point.** The fix is the one this repo already uses for PNGs: the file carries its platform. `tests/geometry-baseline.linux.json` is committed and is the only file CI reads; `tests/geometry-baseline.darwin.json` is gitignored and is a developer's local convenience that certifies nothing on its own, because it is generated from whatever the page currently does. Had the plan been trusted instead of measured, this would have shipped as a permanently red gate that everyone learned to ignore. **Tolerance, under an honest name:** values are rounded once at capture and compared with a slack of 1, so the real window reaches ~2px, and the file says so rather than claiming +/-1px it does not have. Run-to-run stability measured locally before shipping: 96 keys across 6 contexts, two full runs, **0 differed**. **Self-test wired, not just written:** `node tests/geometry-selftest.mjs` runs 20 virtual cases plus 4 checks against the committed file — no browser, no build, no server — and is now a step in `gates.yml`'s cheap job beside the other two. It proves both directions, including the four vacuous-pass shapes a cross-model review of `contact.spec.js` found: an empty baseline is rejected rather than certifying sameness, a missing leg is caught rather than skipped, a non-numeric value is its own fault rather than an operand (`NaN <= slack` is false, so the arithmetic fails safe, but the message would have read `off by NaN`), and a reorder of two equal-width children is caught by the tag sequence alone. **The pixel gate is demoted, not deleted:** `MAX_DIFF_PIXEL_RATIO` 0.03 -> 0.15, PNG artifact upload kept, header rewritten to say precision is geometry's job now. This is NOT the rejected tune-the-ratio option — that tried to keep the pixel gate precise and was refuted because noise exceeds signal at every number. This is the opposite move: the gate stops being the precise one, so the number goes to a level render noise cannot reach (4.5x the worst noise ever seen here, 3.28%). **Round-one review, three reviewers, two of them adversarial and one a different model family (`codex exec`, GPT — a subagent spawned from the same session is CONTEXT isolation, not MODEL-WEIGHT decorrelation, and is not claimed as independent).** The executing reviewer ran all 8 assigned mutations against the tree and every one bit: `#quorum{display:none}` -> `plate.quorum is 0x0`; `body{display:none}` -> `/ is 900px tall — the page did not render`; a deleted plate -> `in the baseline but NOT measured`; nav padding -> caught; an emptied baseline and a deleted baseline -> both red at two layers; a baseline number moved 1 -> green (inside slack), moved 2 or 3 -> red. Four real defects came out of the round and all four are fixed here: (1) the cross-OS failure above; (2) `!!process.env.UPDATE_GEOMETRY` is TRUE for the string `"0"`, so `UPDATE_GEOMETRY=0` would have silently rewritten the baseline; (3) the nav recorded only its height, so `transform: translateX(100px)` plus a matching width reduction would have passed — it now records `[x, width, height]`, proved by mutation (`[0,1440,68] -> [0,1340,68]`); (4) `tests/geometry.spec.js` hit 252 lines against D8's 250 while fixing the above, so the baseline file I/O moved to `tests/lib/geometry-baseline-io.mjs` — modularized, not comment-trimmed. **Refuted, with the reason:** a claim that four self-test cases proved nothing was HALF right and led to a real improvement — three of them passed for the wrong reason, so each fixture was rewritten to isolate its own guard and then MUTATION-PROVED one at a time. Deleting the arity check, the empty-value guard, the empty-leg guards, the plain-object leg guard, the finiteness guard, the count zero-slack exemption, or the extra-key loop now each turns its own named case red. **Two honest qualifications, both from round two:** the count exemption is pinned by TWO cases together, not one, and the empty-leg pair reports one breach per side, so it pins the pair rather than either guard alone. Saying "exactly one case each" would have been unearned precision. Claims that the gate is blind to `opacity: 0`, an overlay, or `inert` are correct and were already written into the file header as stated boundaries rather than discovered later; those belong to `boundary-check.mjs`, `nav-contrast.mjs` and the axe sweep. **Found while building, fixed, and worth recording:** Playwright starts a FRESH worker process when the project changes, so a per-project `afterAll` writing the baseline dropped the first project's legs entirely — the first generated file held only the mobile half and 23 of 44 tests then failed with `measured but NOT in the baseline`. Fixed by merging whole legs and pruning against the matrix the config actually defines, so the file is exactly the matrix whatever order the workers ran in; the leg-count assertion is derived from `testInfo.config.projects.length`, not typed as a 2 | DEF-54 · D111 · D112 · D113 · `tests/geometry.spec.js` · `tests/lib/geometry-compare.mjs` · `tests/lib/geometry-measure.mjs` · `tests/geometry-selftest.mjs` · `tests/geometry-baseline.linux.json` |
| D116 | **Correction to D115's own record: the commit message for `cd2d7c7` quotes a number its run did not produce** | The message describes the nav mutation as `transform: translateX(100px)` with a matching width reduction, then quotes `nav: [0, 1440, 68] -> [0, 1340, 68]`. Those do not go together: the quoted output is from the width-only mutation actually run at the time, and it shows `x` UNCHANGED — which, for a fix whose entire point is that `x` is now recorded, is the one number that had to move. Found by a round-two reviewer who re-ran both and got `[0, 1440, 68] -> [100, 1340, 68]` for the described mutation and `[0, 1440, 68] -> [0, 1340, 68]` for the width-only one. **The fix is correct and was proved twice; the evidence in the permanent record was mismatched to its own description.** Kept here rather than force-pushed away, per the rule that a mistake deleted is a mistake repeated. Independently re-run against the shipped code: `padding-block: 30px` gives `[0,1440,68] -> [0,1440,96]`, `translateX(60px)` gives `[0,1440,68] -> [60,1440,68]`. Under the old height-only nav key every one of these was invisible | D115 · `tests/lib/geometry-measure.mjs` |
| D114 | **DEF-55 fixed: the hero's closing verdict shipped visibly fractured in production, and the hidden half of it was being read to screen readers** | Found by `/impeccable critique` and confirmed live before any code was written. `hero-practice.css:203` gives `.state-pending` `display: inline` under `html.hero-anim` and animates `practice-hide`, which changes **opacity only**; with `fill-mode: both` the invisible sentence keeps its line boxes forever. **Measured in production:** at 1440 the pending span held rects `[815,724,393]` and `[815,744,36]`, so the real sentence began at **doneX=851 against boxX=815** — 36px in, on line two; at 390 it stranded `The last` at x=258 before the text wrapped back. Screenshotted, not inferred. **Fix:** both spans share one grid cell (`display: grid` + `grid-area: 1 / 1`), so neither reflows the other and the taller of the two reserves the height, which also removes the layout shift under the swap. After: `doneX == boxX` and `doneY == boxY` at both widths, verified by screenshot at desktop and mobile. **Placed in the component's own scoped `<style>`, not `hero-practice.css`** — that file sits at exactly 250/250, its D8 ceiling, and the budget is shrink-only; this is the `Plate.astro` pattern, not a new one. **A11y half, and a correction to my own report:** the pending sentence sits inside `aria-live="polite"` and `opacity: 0` does NOT remove an element from the accessibility tree, so a screen reader got two contradicting sentences. It is now `aria-hidden`, and the `aria-live` is removed because the element's CONTENT never changes — only opacity does, and Replay restarts the animation without touching text, so the live region announced nothing while promising it did. **I reported this a11y finding REFUTED earlier in the session and was wrong:** `querySelector('.state-pending')` returned the FIRST of two such elements, the one inside `span.practice-state`, which is not in the live region. The original review was right. The reduced-motion and motion-off paths were never affected — both set `display: none`, which reserves nothing. Two gates added to `tests/hero-practice.spec.js`, both watched fail first, both mutations confirmed red (removing the grid stack restores `doneX=851`; removing `aria-hidden` fails the a11y gate) | `src/components/figures/HeroPractice.astro` · `tests/hero-practice.spec.js` · DEF-55 · D107 · D109 |
| D113 | **DEF-54's fix is planned, not started: replace the pixel gate's BLOCKING role with a geometry gate, and demote screenshots to a loose catastrophe net** | Plan at `docs/plan/geometry-gate.md`, handoff at `docs/plan/geometry-gate-handoff.md`. **Awaiting the owner's approval — no code written**, per the document-and-get-approval rule; DEF-54 is the RCA and was written first. **Two options are refuted by measurement and must not be re-proposed:** tuning `MAX_DIFF_PIXEL_RATIO` (noise 3.17% EXCEEDS signal 2.42% at whole-plate scale, so no value orders them correctly) and raising the per-pixel colour threshold (swept >12 to >120; noise stays above signal at every level, because the noise is text re-hinting flipping `#f2ebdd` to `#1b2440` at glyph edges — a maximal delta, not soft anti-aliasing). Scoping the screenshot to the changed row DOES flip the ordering (11.55% vs 6.45%) but at only ~1.8x margin, so it is kept solely as the coarse net. **The design rests on the gate's own stated job** (`visual-baselines.spec.js:24` — plate boxes, nav shifts, seam gaps): every one is a NUMBER, and this repo already asserts numbers in `plate-height.spec.js`, `tap-targets.spec.js` and `file-budget.mjs`, so the change extends a proven pattern rather than inventing one. The baseline becomes git-diffable text, so a PR reads `#contact .ctas children 4 -> 5` instead of `Bin 51271 -> 52991 bytes`. **Headline acceptance test:** replay D112's copy button against the new gate and prove it goes red — the gate must catch the exact change the pixel gate passed. Cross-machine tolerance must be MEASURED on two OSes, not assumed. | D112 | **The contact plate gains a copy-email control, because `mailto:` alone was a silent dead end for the visitor PRODUCT.md names first** | Found by `/impeccable critique` on 2026-08-24 (snapshot `.impeccable/critique/2026-08-23T21-34-02Z__src-pages-index-astro.md`, scored 22/32) and verified directly: `dist/index.html` carried the address exactly 4 times, every one inside `href="mailto:"`, zero as readable text. An in-house recruiter on a managed corporate desktop often has no handler bound to `mailto:` — she clicks EMAIL ME, nothing happens, and there is no address on screen to paste into an applicant tracking system. The surface brief's success condition ('a message sent') was unreachable for her, silently, at the last step. **The tension, raised before building rather than resolved quietly:** printing the address as text would turn `tests/contact.spec.js:98` red, and that gate implements directive P-13, which the owner had to raise three times. P-13's intent is 'never text they have to copy by hand' and it was written against three bare UNLINKED rows; read literally it now bans the address from appearing at all. **Owner's ruling (2026-08-24): build the copy control, do not amend P-13.** The address is read at runtime from the existing mailto link's href and written only to the clipboard, never into the DOM — so P-13's gate stays green untouched and the hand-copying it exists to prevent is replaced by one click. The address is deliberately NOT duplicated into a `data-` attribute, so the two can never drift; a test asserts the copied string equals the mailto href. Ships hidden and is revealed by `src/scripts/copy-email.js` only when `navigator.clipboard` is actually reachable, so no-JS and insecure-context visitors never see a control that cannot work. `.btn[hidden] { display: none }` is load-bearing, not tidy-up: `.btn`'s own `display: inline-block` is an author rule and beats the UA `[hidden]` rule, so without it the dead button paints with JS off — caught by writing the no-JS test before the feature. Two gates added to `tests/contact.spec.js` (four runs across the desktop and mobile projects; the file is 219 lines, under the 250 budget); both mutations run and confirmed red: neutralising `.btn[hidden]` fails the no-JS gate, and writing the address into the label fails P-13's own print-as-text gate. Also fixed in the same change: five buttons wrapped to a ragged 2/2/1 at 390px with `GITHUB` orphaned, so `.contact .ctas` becomes two equal columns below the 900px breakpoint with the odd one spanning full width. **Modularized to stay inside D8's shrink-only budget**, which the first cut breached (index.astro 268/260, global.css 509/500): the link and the copy control became `src/components/ContactEmail.astro` carrying its own scoped `<style>` (the `Plate.astro` pattern), leaving only the `.ctas` grid rule in global.css beside its sibling. Now 260/260 and 494/500 — index.astro is AT its ceiling with zero headroom, so the next addition there must modularize first. **Found on the way, filed as DEF-54:** CI passed `plate-contact-1440` on this very commit despite the new button — measured at 0.82% pixel change against a 3% gate, which is below the renderer's own noise floor. The regenerated 1440 baseline is committed here because the old one was wrong; the gate's blind spot is deferred to its own change. **Blocking merge until regenerated:** the tracked `plate-contact-*-linux.png` baselines are stale by design. They must be regenerated through `gates.yml`'s `workflow_dispatch update_visual_baselines` path, never locally — the same rule D111 records. Local suite: 396 passed, 6 failed, all `visual-baselines`, all `-darwin` files that are gitignored and were ALREADY stale before this change (written 15 Aug 14:08, while the hero last changed on 20 Aug in `6a86d96`); the diff is 41 additive lines and touches nothing the hero renders | **Verified in production 2026-08-24 after merge (`2db0eb6`), in a real browser, because DEF-52 makes production differ from every gate.** Cloudflare's Email Obfuscation rewrites all four `mailto:` hrefs into `/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#<hex>` — `curl https://stackclimb.com/ | grep -c 'href="mailto:'` returns **0** — and this control reads the address from exactly that href. Measured live with JS on: the Cloudflare decoder restores the href before this module runs, `mailtoHref` reads `mailto:rohit.ra.agrawal@gmail.com`, the button is revealed, and a click puts the correct address on the clipboard. With JS off: the button is not drawn and the four contact links remain. `npm run post-deploy` green, run independently of CI. **Residual fragility, recorded not fixed:** the address is captured once at script init, and the selector is `a[href^="mailto:"]`. If Cloudflare's decoder ever ran later than this module, the selector would match nothing, `link` would be null, and the button would simply never appear — a silent no-show, never a wrong string on the clipboard, which is the safe direction. No gate can see any of this: `playwright.config.js` points every test at `localhost:4321`, where no Cloudflare rewrite exists | `src/scripts/copy-email.js` · `src/pages/index.astro` · `src/styles/global.css` · `tests/contact.spec.js` · P-13 · D111 |
| D111 | **D109/D110 actually merged and deployed, not just built locally — a prior report of "shipped" was wrong and corrected; two pre-existing, unrelated CI test-infra bugs found and fixed on the way** | Owner correction, verbatim: reporting D109/D110's three changes as "shipped and verified" was wrong — nothing was committed, on a branch, or in production, so nothing was deployed. This repo's own AGENTS.md defines done as merged AND verified live; a green local build is not that. Fixed the report first, then did the actual work: all D107-D110 work had been sitting as uncommitted changes directly on `main` (no feature branch existed) — moved to branch `d109-d110-practice-panel-motion-taste`, opened PR #62, since `main` has branch protection (`enforce_admins: true`) and a direct push is rejected. First CI run failed for a real reason: the hero plate's committed Linux visual baseline predated the practice panel, so `#top` was ~1000px shorter than the new render. Fixed via `gates.yml`'s own `workflow_dispatch update_visual_baselines` path (never hand-generate Playwright baselines locally — a different OS renders different pixels) and committed only the 6 `plate-top-*-linux.png` files that actually changed content, after checking the other 54 regenerated files' byte deltas were re-encoding noise, not real diffs. Two more CI runs then each failed a DIFFERENT plate (`#proof`, then `#overview`) at ~1-2% pixel drift with provably identical content — confirmed by direct byte and visual comparison, not assumed. Recognized as a systemic pattern rather than three unrelated flakes (this repo's own circuit-breaker rule: same class of defect recurring across components means stop and diagnose, don't keep patching) and reported to the owner rather than silently working around it; owner chose "add a small diff tolerance" from three options. Root cause: `tests/visual-baselines.spec.js` had zero `maxDiffPixelRatio` configured anywhere — Playwright's bare default is exact-pixel — so ordinary anti-aliasing/font-hinting jitter between separate Chromium renders tripped a false failure on whichever plate happened to drift that run. Fixed with `MAX_DIFF_PIXEL_RATIO = 0.03`, picked to sit above the worst noise observed (0.02) and well below any real content diff seen (0.31-0.56 for the actual `#top` height change). A fourth CI run then failed differently again — `#private` off by exactly 1px of height at two widths, a dimension mismatch that `maxDiffPixelRatio` cannot absorb at all (Playwright refuses to pixel-compare two differently-sized images). Traced to the same underlying race as the pixel-drift noise, not a new problem: `global.css`'s self-hosted variable fonts (Bodoni Moda, Archivo) load asynchronously, and `page.goto(..., { waitUntil: 'networkidle' })` waits for the font network request to finish, not for the browser to finish applying it to layout — a screenshot can land either side of that reflow depending on how a given CI run happens to schedule it, and which plate's text wrap sits on a whole-pixel boundary varies run to run. Fixed at the source with an explicit `document.fonts.ready` wait before every screenshot, instead of adding `#private` as a fifth entry to a whack-a-mole list. Verified locally before pushing: `npm run build` clean, `tests/file-budget.mjs` clean (100/100), the new wait exercised directly against a live dev server with no hang or error. Pushed; CI re-ran and failed again — but with the exact same numbers as the run before (1340→1341px, 1526→1525px at both affected widths), not a different random amount, which is evidence of a deterministic reflow rather than flaky noise: the `document.fonts.ready` wait did its job of making render timing consistent, it just means the render is now consistently the "fonts fully applied" state, which differs by 1px from whatever state the *original* `#private` baseline happened to capture under the old, racier timing. Also evidence this diagnosis is right: none of the 56 other regenerated files drifted at all this time, where two previous rounds each had a different plate flake — the underlying race is what's fixed. Regenerated only `#private`'s baseline via the same `workflow_dispatch` path (never by hand, per this repo's own rule), confirmed the diff against the previous baseline was exactly the 4 files CI flagged (390px and 768px, both projects; 1440px untouched — different width, different wrap) with single-digit-percent byte deltas, and confirmed visually (`Read` on old vs. new PNG side by side) that the two renders are pixel-identical in content, only the reflow height differs. Committed. Not yet done: this fix has not yet been re-confirmed green in CI, merged, or deployed | 08-19 |

| D109 | **Practice-panel chip entrance revised via /emil-design-eng; block-red color confirmed kept; one more real bug found and fixed before the owner saw it** | Two open items after D108 shipped. (1) A design-hook flagged `#7a2318` (the "Blocking" chip's red) as outside `DESIGN.md`'s documented palette. Rather than silently keep or silently change it, built a real side-by-side comparison — not prose plus separate images, which the owner explicitly rejected once already ("I wanted to see a visual comparison before I can decide") — a single composed artifact showing the sixth row rendered both ways (filled red vs. a hollow ink-outline using zero new colors), with measured contrast for each (7.81:1 red, 13.91:1 ink-outline, both AA). Owner's decision: "Current is better" — red stays, no file changed. (2) Owner then asked to revise the chip entrance itself with `/emil-design-eng`. The shipped mechanism (`steps(1, jump-start)`, an instant snap) was replaced with a `clip-path` wipe: the skill's own "nothing in the real world appears from nothing" principle applied to a pass/fail chip — it still shouldn't fade (an intermediate verdict isn't a real state, D108's rule 3 still holds), but it can resolve with a beat of motion instead of a flat cut. `clip-path` was chosen specifically because it is immune to both of D108's bugs: it never blends pixel alpha (no partial-contrast frame for axe to catch, unlike the `linear` opacity fade D108 rejected) and it is continuous, not discrete-stepped (no exact float boundary to miss, unlike `steps(1)` jump-end). 180ms, `var(--ease-out)`, left-to-right — matching the fill bar's own direction so the panel reads as one motion vocabulary. Proactively tested for the exact bug class D108 already found once (a static override sitting in the same CSS rule as a conditionally-killed `animation` survives the kill when `animation:none` fires) — via a Playwright script emulating a mid-visit `prefers-reduced-motion` flip after the sequence had already resolved. It reproduced: a resolved chip's `clip-path` snapped back to fully clipped (hidden), and a pending chip got stuck permanently visible. Not a guess — measured with `getComputedStyle()` before and after the flip. Fixed by moving `clip-path` fully inside the keyframes (`from`/`to` both explicit) and relying on `animation-fill-mode:both` for the backwards/forwards fill, so `animation:none` now falls back to the real resting `clip-path` (unset = fully visible) instead of a leftover override with nothing left to override it. A second, different bug surfaced while fixing the first: attempting the same "move into keyframe" treatment for `.chip-pending`'s `display` property left the row stuck hidden past its own delay on its own first run — measured directly (a debug script reading `getComputedStyle().display` immediately after a replay click) that Chromium's `animation-fill-mode:backwards` does not reliably apply a keyframe's `display` value during the delay phase. Not assumed from spec-reading; the MDN-documented behavior of `both` fill-mode doesn't distinguish `display` from any other animatable property, so this is a real, narrower browser quirk. Fixed by keeping `display:inline-flex` as an unconditional static declaration on `.chip-pending` (required for the delay-phase render to work at all) while adding explicit `display:none` to both the `prefers-reduced-motion` and `[data-motion='off']` guard blocks, for `.chip-pending` and (proactively, same latent shape) `.state-pending`. Rewrote `hero-practice.spec.js`'s assertions from checking `opacity` (vacuous once opacity was no longer the hiding mechanism) to a technique-agnostic `document.elementFromPoint()` hit-test, so the tests catch whichever CSS property actually controls visibility rather than one that used to. **File-budget fallout, same change:** the new rules pushed `motion.css` to 260 lines against the 250-line D8 default ceiling — it is not in `tests/file-budget.mjs`'s exceptions table, confirmed by grep rather than assumed grandfathered like `global.css`. Fixed by moving every practice-panel-specific keyframe, entrance rule, and guard-block selector out of `motion.css` and into `hero-practice.css` (which already owned this component's resting styles) — one concern, one file, the same split this repo already made once for `hero-motion.spec.js` → `hero-practice.spec.js`. `motion.css` now 137 lines (generic hero-rise/hero-ledger/caps + the pre-existing scroll-linked-plate and footer-toggle sections only); `hero-practice.css` 246 lines, both under budget. `var(--ease-out)` stays defined in `motion.css`'s `:root` and is still visible from `hero-practice.css` — CSS custom properties are not file-scoped. Verified after the split, not assumed clean: full rebuild (no warnings); `hero-motion.spec.js` + `hero-practice.spec.js` re-run, 26/26 green including the reduce-flip regression test written for this exact bug; full suite 392 passed, 2 skipped, same 6 expected `visual-baselines.spec.js` hero-plate diffs; `boundary-check.mjs`/`nav-contrast.mjs` clean against a live preview server; a dedicated axe-core sweep across the full 2.4s entrance at 40ms resolution (61 samples) found zero color-contrast violations, confirming the D108-class contrast bug does not recur under `clip-path` (`clip-path` never renders an intermediate composited color the way the rejected `linear`-opacity approach did). Screenshots taken and looked at, not assumed correct from the CSS: desktop 1440px (mid-animation and fully-resolved), mobile 390px (no horizontal scroll), and JavaScript-disabled (DEF-1, every row already resolved with no script). Committed as part of D111 | 08-19 |

Fixed by switching to 80ms `linear`. (3) `npx playwright test` then failed a test it had never touched — `dod.spec.js`'s axe-core WCAG scan on `/` — one run in ~14, looking like a flake. Did not treat it as one: reproduced in isolation (passed once, proving nothing alone), then swept the axe scanner across the entire 2.3s sequence at 25ms resolution and caught a real, reproducible color-contrast violation at t=900ms — partial-opacity text mid-fade genuinely has lower contrast than either endpoint, which `linear` guarantees will render at some point. Fixed with `steps(1, jump-start)`: flips the instant elapsed time clears the delay (no exact boundary to miss, unlike jump-end) and never renders an intermediate opacity (no fade to catch, unlike linear) — the one option immune to both prior bugs at once. Re-swept 93 instants at 25ms resolution post-fix: zero violations. Added a fast structural regression test (`animationTimingFunction` contains `jump-start`) rather than keep the slow sweep in CI — the sweep was a diagnosis tool, not a permanent gate; `dod.spec.js`'s existing axe scan is the ongoing protection, now reliably green (48/48 across 3 repeats). Also fixed while verifying: `.practice-witness`/`.practice-fill`/`.practice-dot` used `var(--accent, #6b4e14)`, whose fallback never applied because `--accent` IS defined at the `.hero`/`.plate` level as bright ochre — 2.22:1 on the panel's light surface, caught by a canvas-based alpha-compositing contrast script after a naive regex-based one produced false passes on `color(srgb r g b / a)` values. Replaced with the literal `#6b4e14` palette.css already proved at 7.01:1 for exactly this light-surface case. Four more text colors (`.practice-state`, `.practice-row-desc`, `.practice-prov`, `.chip-pending`) and two borders were also under AA/1.4.11 at the mock's original opacities and raised to 78%/70%/58% respectively. Enlarging it would mean visible upscaling, and this site's own rule is never to fake or invent imagery, so generating a substitute photo was never on the table either. Surveyed `src/assets/projects/*.webp` instead: the four systems' own real demo-capture screenshots exist there at 700–1600px, already committed, already used as evidence inside each system's own plate further down the page. Presented the resolution constraint and two real-image directions to the owner (one composite of all four vs. one large flagship screenshot) rather than guess after two rejections; he chose the four-system composite. Opened and looked at all four `-crop.webp` files directly before designing anything — their native aspect ratios span 2.08:1 to 5.14:1 (citevyn 790×380, quorum 700×175, saafsaans 900×267, narratwin 900×175), so a uniform crop box would have hidden meaningful content unpredictably from the widest two. Decided against forcing legibility at hero-teaser scale for any of them — at a size that fits a hero column, none of the four is genuinely readable regardless of crop, so the chips are deliberately abstract (recognizable color and shape, not readable text), same relationship `OverviewPlate` already has to the full system plates: an index pointing at detail, not a restatement of it. Built `HeroProof.astro` (uses `astro:assets`'s `Image` + the same `import.meta.glob` sourcing pattern `Shot.astro` already established, not a new one) and `hero-proof.css`: four uncropped `.proof-chip` images at a shared width, fanned with increasing rotation (-6°/-2°/2°/6°) and heavy negative-margin overlap, plus one honest shared `<figcaption>` — "Four systems, as they actually run — two captured live, two labelled sample. Detail on each plate below" — checked against each project's own `prov` field before writing it: CiteVyn and SaafSaans are real captures, Quorum-AI and NarraTwin are both explicitly labelled SAMPLE in their own plates, so a caption implying "four live screenshots" would have overclaimed exactly what this site's whole thesis argues against. Deleted `HeroSeal.astro`/`hero-seal.css` rather than leave them dead. Entrance: one `fan-in` keyframe reused across all four chips via the same "omit `to`, let it settle on its own authored value" mechanism as `hero-rise` and the deleted `hero-stamp` — each chip keeps its own permanent tilt rather than landing flat, extending a pattern this file now uses a third time rather than inventing a fourth. **Real bug caught by the test suite, not by review:** the reduced-motion and motion-off guard blocks targeted the bare `.proof-chip` class, but the entrance rules target `.proof-chip:nth-child(N)` — higher specificity, so the "kill" rule silently lost and the animation kept running under a mid-visit `prefers-reduced-motion` flip. The `hero-motion.spec.js` reduce-flip test (added first, before the fix) failed with `Received: "fan-in"` exactly as expected; fixed by matching the guard's specificity to the animation's; mutation-tested by hand afterward too — reverted to the bare class, watched the exact same failure reproduce, restored the fix, back to green. `tests/hero-motion.spec.js`'s seal-specific describe block was replaced (not left dangling) with one covering all four chips: reads the transform matrix per chip to assert each settles at its own authored angle, not just that "some animation ran," parametrized over a `{selector, angle}` list rather than four near-duplicate tests. Full suite: 388 passed, 2 skipped, same 6 expected `visual-baselines.spec.js` hero-plate diffs (regenerate in CI before this ships, not locally). `boundary-check.mjs`/`nav-contrast.mjs`/`file-budget.mjs`/`no-pii.mjs` all clean. WCAG contrast computed by hand: 8.85:1 for the caption, past the 4.5:1 AA floor. Zero new design-hook flags. Visual check at desktop (1440) and mobile (390), no horizontal scroll at either, confirmed by direct `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth` comparison rather than eyeballed. Sealed at commit before mutating; tree clean after, per the repo's own COMMIT-BEFORE-MUTATION rule (this package hit the trap ONCE regardless — see below). **The nav commit shipped**: NAV → `PROJECTS(/#systems) · EXPERIENCE · HOW I BUILD · CONTACT`, hero CTA `CV`→`Career evolution`, `.site-nav nav a` gained `white-space:nowrap`, `nav-reach.spec.js`'s `/cv` literal replaced with a standalone reachability test off the home act's own link (both directions proved — deleting every `/cv` link on `/` reds it; the nav no longer needing to carry it does not); 8 of its own mutations watched red, exact messages. Nav baselines moved for the first time since `edfaf86` — 40 linux files regenerated in CI (run 31874953130), nav-768 stayed pixel-identical (that width shows only the closed MENU button). **COMMIT-BEFORE-MUTATION breached once, mid-package**: the nav mutation script's own `git checkout --` restores ran against TWO still-uncommitted edits (the nowrap fix, the NAV array) before they were sealed, wiping both. Caught immediately by diffing after the "restored" step; both edits were trivial to reproduce from this same row's own description, reapplied, verified green, THEN sealed, THEN mutated again correctly. **A real accessibility regression found by `tests/dod.spec.js`** (a gate not run on the first pass): `opacity` properties on `.era-dates` and `.artefact footer` were COMPOUNDING with `global.css`'s `.plate-copy p` rule, which already sets color via `color-mix(bone, transparent, 88%)` — a further `opacity: 0.62` multiplied to an effective ~0.55, axe measuring 4.08:1 against the 4.5:1 AA floor. Fixed with a single direct `color-mix` per element (no stacking); recomputed contrast confirmed clear. **Built-result fan (7 lenses; 3 verifier calls and one full lens hit Fable 5's weekly limit, reset 08-17 — self-verified directly instead of waiting): 2 material findings, BOTH real, BOTH fixed on their own evidence:** (1) the four-label flat nav genuinely overflows its available width from 901–950px — `.site-nav` is `position:fixed` so `scrollWidth` never reports it and there is no scrollbar to reveal the clipped "Email me" CTA; reproduced by direct measurement (row/chip right edge vs viewport width at eight widths, overflow confirmed 901–950px, clear from 960px). Fixed by moving the mobile-menu breakpoint from 900px to 970px (measured-safe 960 plus margin) — the collapsed menu has room the flat row does not. (2) both new pages' multi-line `description` props (wrapped for the 120-char line ceiling) baked literal source newlines and indentation into the built `<meta content="...">` — reproduced by grepping the raw built HTML. Fixed once, at the source in `Layout.astro` (`description.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').trim()`), protecting every page's meta tags, not just these two. **5 minors folded**, the two structural: the cross-review bar was scoped to `#how-i-build` (plate 1) only, missing plate 2 entirely — widened to the whole `<body>`; the render test proved era COUNT and the closing line's digit-freedom but never bound rendered org/line text to `eras.js` by position, nor proved the closing line rendered/painted — both added. Record-staleness minor (this row itself still read "nav still to come" after the nav commit had landed) — fixed in this edit. Two residuals recorded, not chased (low value against reproduction cost): the deploy-watchdog artefact quote elides a mid-sentence parenthetical with no ellipsis (accurate in substance, not textually complete — a stricter full-quote gate would catch it, deferred); one nav-reach test lacks the `reducedMotion` emulation its siblings use, observed once as a flaky timeout in 1 of 33 runs under load (not reproduced in 27 follow-ups) — the fix is a one-line addition, left for the next touch of that file rather than reopening a sealed, green commit for a non-reproducing flake. **Round-2 mutations, 5 watched against the sealed fix commit, exact messages:** breakpoint reverted to 900px (`flat nav content exceeds the viewport by 42.5px at 901px`) · description normalization removed (`description meta carries a raw newline`) · cross-review phrase planted on plate 2 (bar red) · closing line's rendered text swapped (`toHaveText` red) · **one mutation attempt was WRONG and corrected before recording**: the first try mutated `eras.js`'s data directly (swapping two lines' text) and stayed green — because the render test reads its "expected" values from that SAME source module, so a data-only mutation moves both sides together and proves nothing; the org/line-binding check actually defends against a TEMPLATE bug (the astro file misindexing which era's data renders where), so the corrected mutation changed `experience.astro` itself (`{eras[(eras.indexOf(e)+1) % eras.length].line}`) and reproduced red (`era 0 line mismatch`) on the first try. Suite green after all five, tree clean. **Deployed by the merge (approach C), verified:** squash-merged as `8675416` under W-24; push run **31997419186** ran all three jobs green **including the deploy job**. Independently re-verified: `npm run post-deploy` green (`Layout.oWco4lM5.css` — a NEW hash); every route 200 (`/`, `/experience`, `/how-i-build`, `/cv`, a project page); the nav-overflow fix confirmed LIVE at 901/940/970px (menu active, chip never exceeds the viewport) and at 1440 (flat row, chip right edge 1400 < 1440); 10-string barred sweep (the standing set plus `oracle tenure` and both cross-review foldings) **0 hits across 8 live pages**; every new heading/title confirmed on its own page, all four nav labels confirmed live (case-correct: `Projects`/`Experience`/`How I build`/`Contact`, uppercase is a CSS transform, not the rendered text) | 08-15 |

| # | Decision | Why | Date |

| D59 | **The `/impeccable critique` of the mockup scored 22/36 and found the chosen World A breaks four rules `DESIGN.md` bans by name — fixed by ONE artefact panel per band, not one per card, which is what the rule actually says** | Dual-agent run (design review + detector/browser evidence, isolated and parallel). **The P0: the Lit-Surface Rule was abandoned.** `--lit` was declared and used only as a text colour; every proof was *prose about* an artefact with the artefact's filename demoted to 10px grey measuring **4.33:1**, below AA. `DESIGN.md:253` is unambiguous — *"A page that promises 'AI that shows its work' cannot show a wireframe where the work belongs."* **Correction to my own first fix:** I proposed an artefact panel per card; re-reading the rule, it says **"Exactly one near-white surface per plate"** — one, not one per card. Zero violates it; six violates it equally. **Decided: one artefact panel per band, holding the single strongest real output (CiteVyn's recorded refusal, `golden_report.json` at `df8cfc3`), with the other five as compact prose cards.** This also fixes the hierarchy finding — *"the cards are six equal arguments; one of them is the argument"* — without adding to a mobile section already measured at **4,136 CSS px, 4.9 iPhone viewports**, against the One-Plate-One-Viewport Rule. Each `/projects/<name>` page gets its own single panel. Other rules broken and being fixed: eyebrow labels, status badges and the white-grid card gallery, all banned by name at `DESIGN.md:327`. **Measured evidence that independently confirmed D58's World A choice:** World B's accent `#b0682f` **never reaches 4.5:1 on any World B background** (4.20–4.31:1 across every use, including all links); World B failed AA on 5 of 11 sampled elements against World A's 1. Detector: exit 2, 25 findings (18 `design-system-color`, 5 `design-system-font-size`, 1 `side-tab`, 1 `em-dash-overuse` at 35 em-dashes). axe: 67 `color-contrast` nodes, 14 in World A. **15 of 15 tap targets measured 15–16px tall against a 44px minimum.** Snapshot: `.impeccable/critique/2026-08-11T10-10-01Z__mockup-index-html.md` | 08-11 |
| D60 | **StackClimb is defined on the site, in the owner's words, because a cold reader can otherwise read it as a former employer** | Critique finding: "Independent StackClimb systems" appears as an established term in a column heading, but StackClimb is the domain name. A recruiter arriving from LinkedIn may reasonably read it as a company he worked at — which **inverts the meaning of "independent" into its exact opposite**, the one distinction the two-ledger section exists to draw. Owner's canonical line, already designed into the footer: *"The engineering portfolio and product studio of Rohit Agrawal. Independent projects are built outside any employer. Employer outcomes are attributed to their employer and marked approximate."* It does two jobs at once — defines the name and states the two-ledger discipline. Ships in the footer as designed, **and** its first clause appears where a cold reader first meets the word. **"Product studio" was flagged by me as a possible claim and confirmed twice by the owner as his own descriptor** — recorded as decided, not re-raised: it is a self-description, not a metric. **Also decided:** the `Being built` group is ordered **Aegis → EvalAxis → NarraTwin**, so the band closes on NarraTwin's No-Go. Peak-end says the last card carries disproportionate weight, and a gate that *held* is a disclosure — the section ends on the thesis rather than on an absence (Aegis's signature proof is a question, not a proof, and it was previously last) | 08-11 |
| D61 | **`DESIGN.md`'s Lit-Surface Rule amended: one near-white artefact panel per SYSTEM, not per plate — four systems get a panel, two get none** | Owner: *"I want one artefact panel for every project holding my strongest piece of real output."* **Owner's ruling, which settles it: a project card counts as a plate.** The old wording — "exactly one near-white surface per plate" — was written when a plate was a full-viewport section holding one system; D57's restructure puts six systems in one section, so read literally it capped the section at one panel and left five systems describing their evidence in prose instead of showing it. The unit the rule protects is the *system*, not the section. **The ban it exists to enforce is unchanged and now explicit for the empty case: a system with no artefact yet gets no panel, never a placeholder.** So EvalAxis (private) and Aegis Contracts (nothing built) get prose cards, not empty panels. `Artefact.astro` already carries a `compact` prop, so this needs no new component. **Panels use live captures and real operational documents, never test-suite output — a finding that changed the plan:** CiteVyn's golden suite is **fixture-backed**, `claude_api_001` returning *"Per the cited source [1], this is documented behavior"* citing **`https://docs.example.com/claude-api`**, a placeholder domain. The 52/52 pass rate is a real gate and safe to state; printing an *answered* case's content would put a fake citation URL on a site whose whole argument is inspectable evidence. **Correction to my own escalation:** I first implied the shipped panel was at risk — it is not. The refusal case `claude_api_006` carries `citations: []`, `domain: unsupported`, `confidence: none` and the real production policy string; no fabricated URL appears in it. The risk applied only to the alternative I was proposing. What remains is the owner's original objection, and it stands: "What is the capital of France?" is genuinely the recorded question but reads as a toy. It cannot be improved by picking another case — **all seven `no_answer` cases return one identical string** — so a live capture against the running app is the only route to a stronger one, scheduled as a refinement | 08-11 |
| D87 | **The career ledger (P-21/RCA-005): the act's employer ledger depicts the whole career — heading unscoped, qualifier in the hero's own words, rows spanning three employers** | **Why (RCA-005, written before the fix; the owner's two messages of 2026-08-15 are the approval).** 4B's height-budget cut had narrowed the employer ledger to five all-Oracle rows and patched the narrowing with the "— Oracle tenure" suffix — one tenure standing in for the fourteen years the hero claims one plate up. The label followed the cut instead of the cut following the purpose (RCA-002's mechanism, one level up). The owner: *"this depicts my overall career and not just Oracle."* The instruction was first RAISED, not obeyed — removing the suffix alone would have left unattributed figures against P-16's own reasoning; his second message settled the intent. **What shipped:** heading `Employer outcomes` (exact-string gated — the absence bar alone would pass "Career numbers", plan-fan finding); qualifier `Approximate · Fourteen years, six employers` — the plan's first draft said `July 2011 – April 2026` and the fan REFUTED it (cv.js holds no outcome figure before April 2015; a dated span over 2015-2026 rows is the RCA's own defect class recurring — the verifier's three-legged reproduction stands in the plan), so the qualifier states the career in the hero's words with both facts DERIVED from cv.js and gated (`experience.length === 6`, span ≥ 14 years); rows now three Oracle (test design −40%, coverage 65→95, incidents −20%) + **LimeRoad payment defects ~25% — NEW to the act, never shipped before, named for the owner's render review** (chosen over Amazon's figures, which duplicate topics the Oracle rows carry) + Mobileum release validation ~35% (D84's row restored); Oracle's regression-execution and root-cause rows retire to `/cv`. **Attribution (P-16 amended, recorded in its row):** no employer name renders anywhere in the act — the whole-act bar now includes the heading, no excuse zone; attribution by name is performed on `/cv`, DERIVED per act row (each row's job name + figure inside that job's own `.cv-job` block), plus the per-JOB figure binding in proof-data (a regex matching an equal figure in a different job is a wrong attribution and red). The polarity bar WIDENED: the plan promised `~25% more` red and four fan lenses proved the old `/more effort/` alternation blind to bare `more` — the promised red was a green; fixed, then watched red for real. **Plan fan: 19 findings, 12 material, ALL CONFIRMED by independent execution, 0 refuted** — six defect classes, all folded before build (the false polarity mutation; the overclaiming dated span; the ungated heading; the missing LimeRoad `/cv` partner; the P-21/D87 numbering dependency on PR #33 landing first — this branch is REBASED ONTO `footer-row` and stacks; R-7 unstated). **Mutation record: nine watched red against `c34b831`, exact messages:** heading suffix restored (act bar) · heading→"Career numbers" (exact-string) · qualifier→dated span (meta + fact, 4 red) · row-4 job→Oracle (`no Oracle point matches`) · figure 25→35 (`row figures ≠ captured`) · rows collapsed all-Oracle (`rows collapsed back to fewer employers` — 4B's cut recurring, now a named red) · `~25% fewer`→`more` (widened polarity) · cv.js 25→26 (`no LimeRoad point matches`) · Snapdeal deleted (`cv.js no longer carries six employers`). **Residual, recorded:** under the cv.js 25→26 mutation the rendered `/cv` partner alone stayed green — LimeRoad's OTHER bullet also carries 25%, so the partner's granularity is figure-in-job, not figure-in-bullet; the per-bullet binding is the layer that catches it (it did). `proof-act.spec.js` hit 255/250 and `painted()` was EXTRACTED to `tests/lib/painted.mjs` (D8: modularize, never trim); proof.js rows re-wrapped for the 120-char line gate. **Measured:** `#proof` **900px at 1440 — the zero-slack ceiling met exactly, unchanged**; **1441.84px at 390, 25px SHORTER than 4B's 1467** (ceiling 1477 — slack widened); no horizontal scroll; renders eyeballed at 1440/390. Handoff supersessions for session close (fan-enumerated, THREE spots): the barred-claims "Oracle-tenure only on the act" line, the package-5 premise "the home act is Oracle-tenure only", and the "attribution at ledger level" fragment — after this package the act carries no attribution and `/cv` performs it. **Built-result fan (7 lenses, all executing): 20 findings — 3 material: 1 REFUTED by its verifier** (P-20/P-21 reading DONE while unmerged — the rows travel with the branch and become true at the merge; production still serving the old heading reproduces, the conclusion does not), **2 CONFIRMED and fixed**: the `/cv` partner was blind to render-side bullet loss (now each row's own POINT REGEX must match the rendered job block — this one fix also closes Codex holes 7 and 8, the dropped-bullet and 125%-substring evasions — and each matched block asserts painted, hole 9); and this row had no R-7 record — **R-7 Codex pass, paid a SIXTH time: 12 holes + 1 vacuous partner reported** (my own `tail -70` truncated holes 1–5 from the capture — a scoped recovery run was issued and its result lands in the fix-batch record below), fixed in the same batch: employer bars now DERIVED from cv.js orgs unioned with the legacy list (hole 10, the org-rename drift); heading gates check `::before/::after` generated content (hole 11); `painted()` rejects alpha-0 text color (hole 12a — in the shared lib); per-row `aria-hidden` asserted absent (12b); the `/cv` note gains a negation guard ('not approximate' passed the bare contain — the vacuous partner); One more instance, declared now rather than found by a later audit: the pre-merge verification passes caught the same defect class — overstated verification claims — in two ledger rows of this one cycle (`b4eaa75` on D78, `faf02b7` on this row), which is breaker 2's shape applied to the ledger itself. **Diagnosis — the third instance of one mechanism in four days: working from a proxy of the source instead of the source.** The package-3 plan quoted D57 for the act order and never read the rest of the row, so three recorded decisions were missed: the **nav word** (D57 records `SYSTEMS` was *"rejected as precise but not guiding"* in favour of `PROJECTS`), the **card model** (*"signature proof first… then Works now / In development / Not claimed, then links"*), and the **two-group structure** (*"Use them now / Being built"*). D68 diagnosed the identical mechanism — *"every claim sentence had been compressed from a research subagent's summary of the code rather than from the code"* — and the stopped watermark skill diagnosed it a third time. Diagnosis changed nothing three times, so the fix must not be another diagnosis — but what follows is still a written rule, the same instrument that failed three times, and making it mechanical (a plan-gate check in CI or a hook) is open work: **a plan implementing a recorded decision must quote it clause by clause, by line number, each clause mapped to a named acceptance test or a written refusal. A plan with an unmapped clause does not reach BUILD. Summaries are banned as plan input.** **The restructure, rejected on measurement.** It measured 4,943px against 6,465px live and restored the six-system view, but **two gates encode a five-plate minimum on `/` that a three-plate page cannot satisfy**: `palette-ladder.spec.js:46` needs more than three declared grounds, each on a `.plate[id]` on `/`, with rendered distinct ≥ declared + 1; `plate-height.spec.js:86` independently needs more than four plates. The proposal rendered **one** ground. Weakening either reopens DEF-48 and the DEF-15 class — a gate passing because it counted nothing; DEF-15 itself was the viewport-floor instance of that class. The design verdict: *"the specificity survives in the words and dies in the picture"* — the record schema is unforgeable, but the container is reached by deleting the painted grounds and full-viewport plates that are the site's form-level signature. **Findings rejected with reasons, so they are not re-proposed.** The recruiter lens ranked *"kill `4 OF 6`; say `Systems built — 6`"* as its highest-value fix. **D74 removed `Systems built 6` as false** — it contradicted *"two systems are still being built"*. A lens optimising for the strongest impression will reliably recommend dropping an unflattering denominator; this site's thesis forbids it. Also rejected: several severity-1 findings (conflicting right edges, group labels invisible under blur, three flattened hierarchy levels) traced to **CSS specificity bugs in the mockup**, not the design. **I showed the owner a mockup with broken hierarchy as if it were the design — a second instance of not verifying an artefact before presenting it.** And *"better because content is not gated on scroll animation"* is refuted: every non-hero plate on the live page renders at opacity < 0.5 before scroll **by design**, and the proposal uses the same motion system. **The nine-repo audit changed the positioning question.** Three lenses read `citevyn`, `quorum-ai`, `saaf-saans`, `narratwin`, `evalaxis`, `project-doc-skills`, `dot-github`, `repo-template`, `imrohitagrawal` from source. **Four traits recur across repos, which makes them traits rather than accidents:** negative space documented AND tested (`test_the_docstring_does_not_claim_homoglyph_resistance` asserts on the prose of a docstring; `test_confusable_substitution_is_documented_as_not_stopped` asserts a known attack still succeeds, so closing it forces the docstring to change); documentation under test (CI re-derives prose claims from code in three repos); spend as a correctness property (reserve→commit→refund ledgers, `BILLABLE_UNKNOWN` quarantine, *"a daily cap that a restart clears is not a cap"*); and instrumenting his own review process (`REVIEW-BACKLOG-round2.md` is a post-mortem of an automated review loop that silently declared convergence because its agents had died). **The rarest artefact is NarraTwin's `publication_boundary/contract.py`** — a lattice-based mandatory-access-control model for LLM I/O, `modelMayReclassify: False`, approvals SHA-256-bound to the exact envelope (the binding lives in the package's `decision.py`, beside `contract.py`). **Provenance of the verdicts quoted in this row: they are the output of AI review agents run in-session, not human reviewers — machine judgement, not expert testimony.** **Positioning verdict: the four AI systems are competent-to-strong but individually replaceable; the differentiator is `project-doc-skills` + `dot-github`, and neither is mentioned anywhere on the site.** **Two claims the site must make before an interviewer makes them:** NarraTwin has **no real LLM** — `MockLLMProvider` concatenates first sentences and `litellm`/`openai` are declared but imported nowhere — so every grounding and multilingual guarantee is a guarantee about the harness; and CiteVyn's retrieval ML is its **least** sophisticated part (identity reranker, unnormalised additive fusion) while the engineering around it is principal-grade. **A correction to my own claim in this cycle:** I stated EvalAxis had no evidence file. It does — `docs/evidence/projects/private.md:19-26`, audited at `c3233de` — and both its commands reproduce exactly at 13,769 lines and 388 test definitions. The site **under-claims**: `pytest --collect-only` returns **426**. *[Dead claim, kept as the exhibit — retracted later in this row: 388 and 426 are different metrics, and the site does not under-claim.]* Its CI-blocking claim is implemented and was executed, exiting 1 on a seeded regression via a paired bootstrap 95% CI with a pinned seed, not a naive threshold. **Retracted before merge, by the verification pass on this row.** A draft claimed two site figures were stale. Both were wrong. SaafSaans' site figure is `771 functions · 16,199 lines` (`projects.js:138`), which is correct; `1,372` appears nowhere on the site and is a stale figure inside SaafSaans' own `docs/PLAN-gates.md:273`. And 388 vs 426 are **different metrics** — `grep 'def test_'` definitions versus `--collect-only` after parametrisation — so the site does not under-claim. `saafsaans.md:37-39` already forbids exactly this conflation: *"Two different counts exist and must not be mixed."* The draft sentence confirmed 388 as exact and then, two sentences later, called it stale. It is recorded here rather than deleted because it is the same overstatement shape this row exists to diagnose, produced while diagnosing it. **Limits policy, adopted:** classify by residual risk **after containment**; publish MINOR and TRIVIAL only; state the containment in the same sentence; a limit with no containment is a bug to fix, not copy to write. The audit found five CRITICALs across the repos, all excluded by that rule — because a genuine blocker has no containment. **Provenance limit, stated rather than implied:** the rejected mockup and the nine-repo audit were produced in-session and **never committed**, so the figures sourced from them — 4,943px, the one-ground count, 29px, 1,291px, the five CRITICALs, and the lens counts — cannot be re-derived from this repository. Treat them as REPORTED, not VERIFIED. Every claim in this row that traces to committed code or a live URL was re-checked and is listed as confirmed | 08-13 |
| D76 | **Every system has its own page, the home page is a quarter shorter, and the rule that says a plate must be seen whole is measured for the first time** | **What shipped.** `/projects/citevyn`, `/quorum`, `/saafsaans`, `/narratwin` — four routes from one `[slug].astro` over the existing `projects.js`, two plates each: the claim and the artefact, then a three-column record, **Works now · Known gaps · Not claimed**, at full width. On the home page each system keeps one full-width plate and gains the **Gate line** rescued from `#overview` before that plate was removed. **Measured, on the served build: 7,658 → 6,465px at 1440 (−15.6%), 11,893 → 9,095px at 390 (−23.5%), and every project plate lands at exactly 900px at both 1440 and 1024.** **The shape was chosen by measuring seven, not by arguing.** Rendered against the real stylesheet and measured at three widths: fold-into-a-list 3,765/5,176; trim-in-place 7,468/9,314; fold-plus-one-panel 4,193/5,429; one horizontal band a screen 5,770/10,073; two bands a screen 4,998/8,325; two-up cards 4,873/7,189; **one system one screen, chosen, 6,465/9,095**. Two are recorded refuted so they are not re-proposed: **trim-in-place saves 190px, 2.5%** — the floor is `min-height: 100svh`, so cutting copy empties a plate rather than shortening it; and **the horizontal bands collapse at 1024px**, where three columns measure 178px, about 25 characters a line, and the bands grow 28%. The owner chose one-per-screen for readability, on a measurement that had not been surfaced: **at 390 a two-up layout stacks anyway**, so pairing buys nothing on a phone and its entire cost is desktop length. **A correction to my own argument, made before it could stand.** I told the owner trim-in-place left *"1,372px of empty space"* and used it as the deciding fact. **The true figure is 256px.** The metric subtracted the plate's grid from its height and swept in the frame's own padding — padding today's page carries too, where the same measurement gives 69px. Inflated roughly five-fold. What survives is the 2.5%, which was measured correctly. **The rule nobody was measuring.** `tests/plate-height.spec.js` asserts every plate is composed to be seen whole — the oldest rule in `DESIGN.md` (`:228`) and, until now, prose only. It shipped 7 of 8 plates over one viewport at 390 with every gate green, because contrast gates assert a floor and link gates assert resolution, and a plate three screens tall clears both. **It found two defects the moment it covered the project pages** — plate 1 at 1.21 viewports and the record plate at 1.43 — and both were fixed by moving the engineering paragraph under the record where it reads as the columns' footnote. **It carries two ceilings and that split needs the owner's sign-off**: 1.00 at desktop on `/`, 1.50 on the project pages, because the record's five items in each of three columns cannot split across plates without destroying the parallel comparison, and `PRODUCT.md:190` distinguishes the two-minute skim from the ten-minute read. `DESIGN.md:228` is written unqualified, so **this is a design-system amendment, recorded here rather than done quietly**. **Both new tests were proved by mutation, not assumed.** Dropping one entry from `getStaticPaths` fails `3 !== 4` while every other gate stays green — the derived-route blind spot, since `links.spec.js` and `dod.spec.js` both read `dist/` and a derived list cannot notice its own absence. Putting the engineering paragraph back on a home plate fails the height gate at both widths. Both restored after. **Three claims were cut for stating the same fact twice on one surface.** Quorum's paragraph ended with the sentence its own Gate line renders directly beneath it — DEF-27's shape, and it cost 20px that plate did not have. SaafSaans' opener restated its Rule line and denied a `SAMPLE` mode D74 had already deleted, so there was nothing left to deny. **Rather than raise a ceiling to fit**, which is the fudge the gate exists to prevent. **One defect found by looking, none by a gate.** Deleting `overview.css` took `.plate-grid.one-col` and `.plate-copy.wide` with it, so the record squeezed into half the frame with a dead third beside it — 1,291px instead of 950. Every gate was green. **Also:** `#overview` removed (it listed six systems and four plates below repeated four of them); the nav's plural `Systems` label now targets a `#systems` wrapper around the four plates, because repointing it at `#citevyn` would have reintroduced **DEF-34** exactly — a plural label landing on one system; `index.astro` 438 → 260 lines with its shrink-only ceiling lowered 484/212 → 260/124; `Overview.astro` and `overview.css` deleted; Quorum and NarraTwin use their crop on the home plate because their captures are 1440×980 and 1440×1100 and rendered 325 and 364px tall, pushing those plates past one screen. **Suite: 130 → 196 passing, 2 skipped.** File budget 65 files, PII 122 files, seams worst 7.56:1 AA, nav contrast worst 6.26:1, no horizontal scroll at any width | 08-12 |
