| D77 | **Two review lenses found eight real defects in D76, including a visible regression I introduced and a gate ceiling that caught nothing — and the fix for the ceiling was a better layout, not a smaller number** | **Round 1 of 2.** One lens ran the pages and reported rendered output; the other was Codex, a different model family. Both were told to break the work. **The regression I caused.** Moving `Not claimed` out of the caption strip took every strip from five cells to four — and `caps.css:19` (`.cap:last-child:nth-child(3n + 1) { grid-column: span 3 }`) is **not scoped to a media query**, so at ≤900px a four-cell strip conjured an implicit third column in a two-column grid and starved tracks 1 and 2 to **29px**. Caption text overprinted itself at 360, 390, 412 and 430. **Five cells had been hiding a rule that was always wrong.** No gate could see it: overflow inside a grid cell never widens the document, and `dod.spec.js:112` measures only `documentElement.scrollWidth`. Fixed by resetting the three-column span rules inside the media query; verified zero overflowing cells at all four widths. **The ceiling that caught nothing.** I set the project-page plate ceiling at 1.50 viewports against a worst real plate of 1.09 — 38% of slack, which is the "measurements plus a margin" fudge the gate exists to prevent, in the other direction. **The reviewer was right and the fix was not a smaller number.** Run through `/impeccable layout`, the diagnosis was structural: the record was **three equal columns for 5 / 5 / 2-3 items** — a framework default, not a shape the content earns. The short column stretched to match its neighbours and carried ~160px of dead space, while the note sat in a band beneath the grid at 476px wide inside a 1,123px plate, leaving **647px empty beside it**. The plate was simultaneously 83px too tall and a third empty: one problem, not two. **Three alternatives were measured in the DOM before any file was edited, and my first hypothesis was wrong** — moving the note into the narrow third column made every plate *taller*, because the note grows more in a 353px column than the void it fills. Full-width note fits at 1,123px but that is a **~100-character measure**, buying pixels by breaking readability. The shipped answer pairs the two genuinely parallel five-item lists in row 1 and the two short elements — what it refuses, and how it works — in row 2. The note lands at **545px, 68 characters**, the measure it wants. Worst record plate **983px → 911px**; the ceiling is now **1.10 against a measured worst of 1.05**, a 5% margin matching the 3-7% the other three carry. **DEF-27 had recurred, on all four new pages.** The record bullets and the engineering note restated each other within one plate — CiteVyn's *"An unstamped legacy index is let through on purpose"* appeared **verbatim, twice**. Eight bullets removed; every claim survives in the note, which carries the reasoning as well. **Both new tests were holed, and both mutations came from the reviewers.** `plate-height.spec.js` counted DOM nodes, not rendered plates, so `.plate { display: none }` passed with every plate at 0px — the same shape as the `body { display: none }` hole a cross-model review found in `contact.spec.js`. And it rounded before comparing, so a 904px plate passed a 900px ceiling as `1.00`. `links.spec.js` used a document-wide locator, so **swapping CiteVyn's and Quorum's hrefs stayed green** while a reader clicking under CITEVYN landed on QUORUM-AI. All three fixed and re-proved by re-running the reviewers' own mutations. **Claims tightened:** *"Two of 21 stations map to no feed"* stated the barred denominator as fact — `saafsaans.md:27` says quoting 21 counts the two absences as coverage — now reads *"Two stations map to no feed at all… 19 are live"*; the five surviving `21 stations` hits all carry the `repo-stated` hedge. *"not a fifth model"* was a **denial** of a barred string, but a grep cannot tell a denial from a claim, so the sentence was rewritten and the string is now **0** in the output. **The page description was the IMAGE alt text** — on Quorum that published the SAMPLE fixture's *"4 of 4 models aligned"* and *"13%"* as a page and social-card description stripped of the caption labelling them canned data. Now derived from the system's own first paragraph, which carries no figures. **Also:** *"All systems"* pointed at `/#top`, the hero, not `/#systems`; `.ov-name a` survived in `touch.css` as a dead selector after its only markup was deleted. **Measured after: home 6,465px at 1440, 6,455px at 1024, 8,996px at 390; worst project plate 1.05 / 1.01 / 1.83 viewports; no horizontal scroll at any width; 196 tests passing; barred-string sweep 0 for MTTD, cycle time, Lighthouse, RAGAS, Arize, mutual critique, critique one another, sample mode, fifth model.** **Residuals, written down rather than chased** (the two-round breaker): the height gate still tests only 1440 and 390, so a defect that inflates a plate only between ~700 and ~1200px is invisible to it — and at 1024×768 the home page already exceeds 1.00 on four plates; plate 1 of a project page remains a near-copy of the home plate minus its Gate line and Not claimed row, so the first screen of the click buys less than the plate it came from; and `pages` → `projects` drift is enforced in one direction only | 08-12 |

And `.proof`'s `margin: 0 0 1rem` shorthand beat `.plate-copy p + p` on specificity, so **the proof line butted onto the paragraph above and read as its last sentence, on Quorum and SaafSaans both**. No gate asserts vertical rhythm; the screenshot did. **Measured:** build clean, file budget 60 files green, seam gate worst 7.56:1 AA, nav contrast worst 6.26:1, PII 113 files clean, no horizontal scroll at 390 or 1440, **124 Playwright tests passing**. **The visual baselines needed regenerating, and two things I first wrote about them were wrong.** **(a) I claimed the delta was "exactly the two hero strip values and nothing else," citing the diff image. That was never established.** `visual-baselines.spec.js:68` awaits each assertion **inside** the plate loop, so the first failure throws and every later plate is **never compared**. Reading the one diff image the run produced told me only that `#top` moved — it could say nothing about the six plates behind it. A reviewer measured branch against `main` directly and found **6 of 7 plates moved**: `saafsaans` +73px at every width, and `overview`, `citevyn`, `quorum`, `narratwin` all shifting at 768 and 390. The copy edits and the `.proof` margin changed heights on nearly every plate, which is exactly what should have been expected. **The lesson is the fail-fast one: a loop of assertions reports the first failure, never the population.** **(b) I wrote that tracked `-linux` baselines "regenerate on the runner (D69)", which reads as automatic. Nothing regenerates them.** `gates.yml:147` gates regeneration on a **manual `workflow_dispatch` with `update_visual_baselines: true`**; an ordinary PR run reaches `gates.yml:134` and compares. So this branch turns CI red until someone runs that dispatch and commits the artifact. **Round 1 also found three more claims, two of them mine.** **(i)** The trimmed Quorum engineering line read *"The eval judge behind the **moderator**"* — I made a vague sentence specifically false. The eval judge is Layer B of the per-run evaluation engine; `evaluation.py:1658`'s `build_judge_evidence` takes `query_text`, `initial_answers` and `final_synthesis` and **never sees a debate round**. The phrase is gone. **(ii)** *"52-case golden run blocks index promotion"* — on `#overview`, on `/cv`, and as a caption strip labelled **Release gate** — is refuted by CiteVyn's own `promotion_eval.py:17`, which calls wiring the golden suite in *"**wrong, and deliberately not taken**"*, because it measures a fixture corpus and *"would attest to a corpus the candidate index does not contain."* The real gate is a separate **15-case retrieval suite run against the CANDIDATE index** at `index_promotion_min_pass_rate` = **0.95** (`core/config.py:310`). Pre-existing copy, but this change staked the lede on it, and **the truth is the better claim** — a system refusing the easier gate because it would certify the wrong thing is this site's whole argument. The evidence file had it right all along; only the site copy conflated them. **(iii)** `docs/evidence/projects/saafsaans.md:95` still listed the deleted `saafsaans-hi.webp` as a live panel asset — the carry-forward audit this repo requires when removing anything. Replaced with a dated removal note rather than a silent deletion. **A build-time guard was added** for the collision a reviewer spotted but no row triggers: `gate` and `rule` share `grid-area: gate`, so a row carrying both would stack them in one cell. `Overview.astro` now throws at build. **Proved it bites** — giving SaafSaans both fields fails the build with `SaafSaans carries both a gate and a rule`, and the clean build was restored. **Considered and declined:** a reviewer read `Systems built — 4 of 6` in the hero as contradicting the overview plate's `Six systems` eyebrow. It does not — six systems exist, four are built, and the private plate says so in as many words. The refuted version was `Systems built — 6`, which contradicted *"Two systems are still being built"* numerically. Recorded so it is not re-proposed. **Round 2 — the cap, per the two-round circuit breaker. Two findings held, three were refuted or declined, and the residuals are written down rather than chased.** **Held (i): "critiques them, twice" is the happy path, not an invariant.** `debate.py:331` skips round 2 when the per-run debate budget is exhausted and records a `debate_round_skipped` event. On a site whose subject is disclosure, stating the count as flat fact was the wrong shape — the copy now reads *"critiques them over two rounds; a round dropped for time is recorded as skipped, not hidden"*, which is both true and the better sentence. **Held (ii): the CiteVyn gate I had just corrected still stated a configurable, bypassable gate as absolute.** `index_versions.py:345` lets `force=True` promote past a failing rate, and 0.95 is a default (`core/config.py:310`), not a constant. The fix again makes the claim *better*: the override is written to the audit trail with the measured rate, on both the clean and forced paths — which the evidence file already recorded and the site had never said. **Refuted: "the fixes add 244- and 185-character lines, exceeding the repository's 120-character ceiling."** `file-budget.mjs:46` carries a deliberate, recorded, shrink-only exception for `src/data/cv.js` at `lineLen: 260`, written for exactly this case — *"long URLs and evidence strings in comments and data."* The executable budget is the authority over the prose ceiling, per D8's own *"budgets must be executable"*. Checked the stricter thing the reviewer did not: whether the change **raised** the file's longest line, since the exception is shrink-only. It did not — **260 on `main`, 260 on this branch.** **Leftovers, shipped rather than chased.** (1) *"reads all four answers"* is not invariant either: `query_runs.py:2113` continues when fewer than four models complete, and the moderator prompt iterates whatever survived. Enumerating every partial-failure path on a portfolio plate is the defensive over-engineering `AGENTS.md` warns about; the plate already discloses that live execution is off by default. (2) The new build guard covers `Overview.astro` only — `/cv` renders from `cv.js` and has no equivalent check. One data shape per page is the real fix, and it belongs to the project-depth package, not here. **Review round 1 refuted two of my own fixes, both found by the cross-model lens and neither by the same-family agents — the exact reason `AGENTS.md` requires a different model family. (a) I replaced a false claim with a new false claim.** The rewritten Quorum sentence said *"A fifth model — a separate moderator"*. It is not provably a fifth **model**: `config.py:434` defaults `debate_model_id` to `anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5`, and `model_slots.py:63` is that same id as **answer slot 2**, annotated in the source as `# slot 2 — Anthropic (debate)`. It is a fifth **call in a separate role**, using the same model by default. Now reads *"a separate moderator pass — its own call, with its own configured model"*. **(b) The correction did not travel — inside this very change.** `/cv` still shipped **all three** refuted claims: `cv.js:143` *"two rounds of mutual critique"*, `cv.js:151` *"deterministic fallback, or sample"*, `cv.js:170` Aegis' *"Too early to state one"* under a **Gate** label. I had grepped for the exact phrases on the home page and not for the **claim**, which is the substring-versus-token failure this file already records twice. It is also, precisely, the failure I had just written this row to describe about another repo. `/cv` and `#overview` now classify identically — **4 Gate, 1 Rule, verified in both built pages** — and `cv.astro`'s section note no longer asserts that every row states a gate, because two do not. **Confirmed by the reviewer, not assumed:** SaafSaans' runtime has no stand-in path left — `waqi.py:244`'s `_fallback()` returns `None` for every numeric field. Its own README and DEPLOY.md still describe a `SAMPLE` mode, which is that repository's debt, not this site's. | 08-12 |
| D73 | **Approach C is written and one secret away: `gates.yml` deploys and then verifies production. The post-deploy gate gained the settle wait and N-of-M rule correction 281 required** | **The deploy job** `needs` both gate jobs and is gated `if: push && ref == main`, so a red gate cannot reach production and a PR build never touches it. It builds, runs `wrangler pages deploy`, waits 20s, then runs `tests/post-deploy.mjs` against the live origin — because **the deploy is not the check**: wrangler printed `Success! Uploaded 13 files (20 already uploaded)` on the run that shipped a 404. **Blocked on one secret, `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`.** Until it exists the job **skips loudly** — a `::warning::` plus a run-summary line saying production was not updated — rather than failing. A skipped deploy that looks like a passed deploy is the precise failure the job exists to prevent, so the absence is made visible instead. **Separately, `tests/post-deploy.mjs` was a bare single-shot fetch — the exact shape correction row 281 forbids** after a seam gate went red-then-green three times on 08-09 with the cause never established. It now states its rule up front and enforces it: a settle wait before the first fetch, and an asset is FAILED only if it fails **all 3** attempts spaced 1.5s apart. A recovery inside the rule is reported as **flapping** and never silently swallowed, because unexplained flapping is a cause, not noise. Both directions proved by self-test: **one transient 503 recovers and reports as flapping (0 problems, 1 flapped); a persistent failure still fails after all attempts.** Eight self-test assertions now pass | 08-12 |
| D72 | **A ledger audit found nine rows wrong, two of them measured numbers — and the audit happened only because a plan overwrite silently dropped half the register** | Three read-only agents were told **not to trust this file** and to verify every row by running a command. **The trigger:** the working plan document had been overwritten once, replacing a full pending register with a five-package plan carrying none. M6, M9, M4, branch protection, `/cv` ResumeSkills, P-12, W-20, DEF-19/21/24/49 and D30 fell out in one stroke, and the `colorize` package was lost entirely. They survived only because this file held them — **luck in the design, not diligence**, and exactly the carry-forward audit `AGENTS.md` requires when consolidating. **The two that mattered most were numbers.** O-30SEC claimed the name renders at 18.24px against a 96px headline. Measured with `getComputedStyle` on the served build: **name 31.68px, h1 59.04px** — 1 : 1.86, not 1 : 5.3. **18.24px is the lede paragraph**, and **96px is a `clamp()` value deleted long ago**, with the replacement and an explanatory comment sitting at `global.css:289`. A row wrong by 1.74× on one figure and quoting a deleted value for the other would have sent the next reader to fix a problem that no longer has that shape. **Also refuted:** O-30SEC's "five job titles" (one ships), O-SKILLGAP (six positioning skills installed — recorded by this file's own D34), M2 (14 of 14 present), I-2 (public since 08-09, and `STATUS.md` had already struck O2 for the identical staleness), W-6 (tracked `.githooks/pre-commit` + CI + enforced protection all exist), W-18 (`AGENTS.md:341` has the rule it says is missing), D62's residual (D66 amended it the same day), D69's "and from disk" (54 darwin files are back, harmlessly). **M6 struck as won't-do:** its observation is true but **DEF-3 records preloading killed on measurement** — desktop LCP +44ms, mobile +96ms, 7-run medians. The two have contradicted each other in this file since 08-09; the measurement wins. **M5 marked unauditable:** striking it replaced its item text with the word "Done", so no claim survives to test. When striking a row, strike the status and keep the item. **I-5's notice rewritten:** every row of the approach-C exit table now reads done, so the debt is no longer *blocked*, merely *not migrated* — a stronger and more actionable statement | 08-11 |
| D71 | **A deploy printed `Success` and shipped a 404. Branch protection is now on and tested, and `tests/post-deploy.mjs` is the first check in this repo that looks at production** | **The defect:** `wrangler pages deploy` reported *"Success! Uploaded 13 files (20 already uploaded)"* while `citevyn-demo.B8TdRMpf.webp` returned **404** on `stackclimb.com`. Cloudflare's content-hash dedup claimed to hold a blob it did not, and wrangler never reads back what it skips, so a wrong answer from the server is indistinguishable from a right one. A second deploy fixed it — 200 at 30,430 bytes. **Why nothing caught it, established by reading the workflows rather than guessing:** every gate runs against a local `astro preview` of a freshly built `dist/`, and the file was *correct* in `dist/`. The failure was in the upload, a step CI does not perform. **Zero deploy steps exist in any workflow, and nothing anywhere fetched production** — the only mention of `stackclimb.com` outside `astro.config.mjs` was a comment about canonical URLs. The page returned 200 the whole time; only fetching each referenced asset found it. **Two fixes, both proved rather than assumed. (1) Branch protection**, closing the last open row of the approach-C exit table: both gate jobs required, strict up-to-date, force-pushes and deletions blocked, conversation resolution required. **It was tested by trying to evade it** — a direct push to `main` was *accepted* while `enforce_admins` was false, and rejected with `GH006` once it was enabled. A gate that has never refused anything has proven nothing, and this one refused its author. One artefact of that test survives in history: empty commit `0cceb01`, which could not be removed because force-pushes are now blocked — the protection working as intended. **(2) `tests/post-deploy.mjs`**, with `--self-test` that plants each failure shape against a fixture server: it catches a 404 asset on a 200 page (the exact 08-11 defect), catches production serving an older build than `dist/`, counts the assets it checked so a checker that finds zero cannot pass everything, and stays silent on a healthy site. Green against production: 11 assets, build stamp matched. Added as **Definition of Done item 7** and `npm run post-deploy`. **Deliberately not put on a schedule.** `STATUS.md:266` already records that a post-deploy gate going red one run in three is worse than none, and with manual deploys a scheduled freshness check would fire constantly. It runs after a deploy, by the person who deployed | 08-11 |
| D70 | **The panels are ported into the real site as components; a review of the port caught six regressions, including a rule I cited in code but never wrote down** | The prototype lived in a git-ignored `mockup/`, so the panel work would have vanished with the directory while only the images and evidence survived. Ported as `src/data/projects.js` (content), `src/components/figures/Shot.astro` (the panel), `src/styles/shot.css` — and `index.astro` **shrank 480 → 420 lines**, so the port bought headroom under its 484 ceiling rather than spending it. **What the review caught, all fixed: (1)** `.proof` shipped with **zero CSS** — the measured-outcome line rendered byte-identical to body copy and its one ochre word came out as plain italic, so the panel's whole emphasis was silently lost. **(2)** `Shot.astro` and `shot.css` both cite **D66** as the authority for the screenshot carve-out, and **D66 had no row in this ledger** — the code depended on a decision that existed only in `DESIGN.md`. Recorded now as D66 below. **(3)** NarraTwin shipped `crop: null` and rendered at **19%** on a 390px phone, the exact failure its own component comment says the crop exists to prevent; SaafSaans' crop rendered **270×20px**, unreadable. Both re-cut — and NarraTwin's new crop is *better* than its full capture, because it frames the disclosure bar (`SIMULATED HOST CONTEXT`, the consent checkbox, `External web disabled by policy`) rather than a workflow table. **(4)** Quorum's real production readiness review — a Go dated 21 June and the No-Go it superseded five days earlier, both kept in the file — was **dropped and replaced by a SAMPLE fixture capture**. On a site arguing for real artefacts, trading a dated real decision for labelled canned data is a regression; restored in prose. **(5)** `19 live · 2 known gaps` had no evidence line; `saafsaans.md` now records that two of 21 `FEED_MAP` entries are `None` because WAQI carries no station for them, so quoting **21** counts absences as coverage. **(6)** NarraTwin's commit stamp was dropped from its strip, which is what makes the count checkable. **Partly refuted:** the review reported EvalAxis and Aegis losing their caption strips — they are on the untouched private plate at `index.astro:337`, grouped differently in the prototype. Their verified `13,769 lines · 388 tests` is still absent from the site, which is an opportunity, not a regression. **Measured after: build clean, file budget 58 files green, axe 0 violations at 1440 and 390, no horizontal scroll, all 8 images content-hashed into `dist/_astro/` (the DEF-24 trap avoided), both `SAMPLE` captions shipping, and zero occurrences of `628`, `MTTD` or `capital of France` in the output.** **Baselines regenerated and CI is green.** `workflow_dispatch` run `31510254419` produced them on the ubuntu-latest/chromium image, downloaded as an artifact and committed unchanged — never hand-made on a laptop (D69). **36 files changed, every one a `plate-*` baseline; zero `nav-*` moved.** That is the signature this change should have: the plates grew taller to hold the panels, the nav did not shift at all, and a changed nav baseline would have meant an unintended regression. Push run `31510575888` then passed the full suite on the runner. **One limitation stated rather than papered over: Codex produced no output in 25 minutes and was killed during session cleanup, so this round had a single review lens, not the cross-model pair I committed to.** | 08-11 |
| D66 | **`DESIGN.md`'s "no photographic texture" ban carved out for artefact panels** | The ban reads *"no second accent, no gradient, no photographic texture; the grain layer is the only material."* Taken literally it forbids the one thing the Lit-Surface Rule demands — *"actual system output at a size you can read"* — because a capture of the running system **is** a photograph of it. The rule that admits it is stronger than the rule that bans it, so the ban now carries one narrow carve-out: **only inside an artefact panel, only that system's own output, never decoration, never a background, never stock photography or a person.** Every such image carries a provenance caption naming its source, and anything that is not a live capture says so there. **This row exists because the code cited D66 before the ledger did** — `Shot.astro:6` and `shot.css:1` both name it, and a port review found the register had no such entry. A decision the code depends on that lives only in a design doc is exactly the drift this file exists to prevent | 08-11 |
| D69 | **I committed 54 laptop-generated visual baselines against the rule that forbids exactly that, and removed them. `.gitignore` now stops it recurring** | `git add -A` after a local `npx playwright test` swept up every `-darwin` snapshot Playwright had just written. `tests/visual-baselines.spec.js:18-21` is unambiguous: *"Baselines are NOT hand-generated on a laptop: they are produced by a CI run on the same ubuntu-latest/chromium image gates.yml uses… and only that run's artifacts are committed."* Shipped in `35afbf0`. **Blast radius, measured rather than assumed:** CI runs Linux and resolves `-linux` baselines, so no gate was broken and the 54 committed `-linux` files are untouched. The damage is subtler — a macOS contributor would then diff against *my laptop's* rendering, so real drift could pass locally while the CI baseline says otherwise, and 54 binary files of dead weight ride along. Removed from the index; the tracked set is back to **54 linux, 0 darwin**. **Correction 08-11: the "and from disk" half of this sentence is no longer true** — a local `npx playwright test` regenerates all 54 `-darwin` files, and 54 sit on disk now. Harmless, because `.gitignore` catches them and `git ls-files` shows 0 tracked, which is the half that matters. Recorded because a row claiming more than it delivers is the defect this session kept finding. **Prevented rather than just fixed:** `.gitignore` now excludes `tests/visual-baselines.spec.js-snapshots/*-darwin.png`, so the next `git add -A` cannot repeat it. **Found by auditing my own merge**, not by a gate — no check exists that fails when a laptop baseline is committed, which is itself worth knowing | 08-11 |
| D68 | **Two review rounds on the rebuilt artefact panels found the same defect class in all four components — fixture data presented as measurement — and the circuit breaker was honoured** | Round 1: three parallel lenses (truth, design, technical). Round 2: `codex exec --sandbox read-only`, a **different model family**, because `AGENTS.md` records that a subagent is context isolation and not model-weight decorrelation. **The breaker fired on rule 2 — the same class of defect in more than two components** — and implementation stopped for the owner rather than continuing to patch. **Diagnosis, per the rule that says name the miss before fixing: an understanding miss.** Every claim sentence had been compressed from a *research subagent's summary* of the code rather than from the code, so each one overstated in the same direction. The fix was to re-read every mechanism at source and rewrite, not to patch sentences. **What the reviews caught, all now corrected:** Quorum's `$0.188 actual · 41.2s` were **hardcoded fixture values** (`e2e/fixtures/golden-run.ts:381,390`) presented in a cell labelled *actual*, with `openrouter_live_execution_enabled: bool = False` by default and the PNG untracked at the named commit — the single worst thing nearly shipped, and it had already reached the ledger in `1ba1249`. CiteVyn's *"every index carries the provider, model and dimension"* was false: `hybrid.py:296-302` documents **three allow-paths**, including unstamped legacy indexes let through on purpose. SaafSaans' EPA claim inverted the truth — `risk.py:77-78` says *"EPA does not publish this mapping — it is our reading"* — and the page claimed a `SAMPLE` mode `presenters.py:571` records as **deleted**. NarraTwin's `BILLABLE_UNKNOWN` sentence was true of `GoogleGeminiTTSProvider` and **false of `ElevenLabsTTSProvider` in the same file**, which retries a timeout with `sleep(); continue` and no ledger; the panel now discloses that gap in its own copy. Quorum's *"two rounds of critique"* read as models critiquing each other, which ADR-0032 exists to prevent. **Round 2 then found the same class again:** `p95 26.2 ms` came from `build/gates/perf-percentiles.json`, stamped `run_id: "local"`, `sha: "local"` — **one laptop run** — while `tests/perf/test_workflow_latency_percentiles.py:28-40` records **ten** runs at **p95 42.2–82.3 ms**. The flattering sample from the least representative run. No latency figure ships, and *latency* is now in that panel's `Not claimed`. Mutation 88.7% also dropped: a partial false positive as raised, but the same test shows CI **declines to compare** it off-platform, and a figure CI will not compare is not one the site should quote. **Refuted, not fixed:** the design lens argued the NarraTwin panel must go under the no-placeholder rule — overruled by the owner, recorded as D67 rather than silently broken. **Measured after:** detector 0 anti-patterns, PII gate green over 108 files, mobile crops added via `<picture>` so each artefact is legible at 390px where full captures rendered at 15–17% scale | 08-11 |
| D67 | **`DESIGN.md`'s no-placeholder clause amended by the owner, over my objection** | The Lit-Surface Rule said a system with no artefact gets **no panel, never a placeholder**. A design review invoked it to remove the NarraTwin panel entirely. **Owner's decision: keep the image, label it `SAMPLE`.** My objection, recorded because it was made and overruled rather than dropped: the site's argument is that its evidence is inspectable, and a design is not evidence. The owner's position, which stands: a labelled design shows what the product does without claiming it runs, and this one carries its own disclosures on its face — `SIMULATED HOST CONTEXT`, `Local mock · no external provider calls, real media, or cloned identity`, `Verified sources · pending`. The rule now carries a narrow exception that does not travel: an interface **the owner built** for **that system**, captioned `SAMPLE` and saying in words it is not a running capture. Never stock art, never a competitor's UI, never unlabelled. Recorded in `DESIGN.md` inline, because a rule the pixels quietly break is drift | 08-11 |
| D65 | **M8 closed: all five evidence files written, every number re-derived at HEAD — and the audit refuted four figures I was about to ship, two of them mine** | `docs/evidence/projects/` now holds `citevyn.md`, `quorum-ai.md`, `saafsaans.md`, `narratwin.md`, `private.md`, each carrying the command and commit SHA behind every number. **Four refutations, recorded in the evidence README's mistakes list so the shape is not repeated:** (1) **"CiteVyn hit-rate 1.0 over 54 cases"** — 54 is the *judge* count; hit-rate 1.0 is over **26 answerable** cases, so the claim more than doubled the scope of a real result. (2) **"CiteVyn zero refusal leaks"** — `retrieval.refusal_leaks` is **5** of 19; only the *answer* layer is clean at `refusal_leaks_judged: 0`. (3) **"NarraTwin answerRelevancy 0.903, contextRecall 0.75"** — the committed `docs/EVAL_REPORT.md` records **1.0** for both; the lower figures came from a research subagent reading a JSON in a worktree since deleted, most likely a different newer run, now unrecoverable. (4) **"Aegis Contracts has no repository"** — mine, from 08-11: it exists and is private, merely not cloned, and *"not cloned"* is a different fact from *"does not exist"*. **The methodological finding, which the owner forced:** my first draft of the prompt to the NarraTwin session quoted the expected values back to it. He stopped it — asking a session to confirm numbers you have handed it invites it to reproduce them rather than report what it found, the same pass-filtered-generator failure this repo already measured on tests. The corrected prompt established existence, source and verification method before asking for anything, and withheld every figure. It came back with the refutation above. **NarraTwin's Lighthouse 100/100/100 is now `UNVERIFIED` and barred from the site** — the harness exists but no result is committed anywhere, `git log --all` on the path returns nothing, and Lighthouse is non-deterministic with no measured variance on record. Its eval figures may ship only with their commit stamp and the *"predates current HEAD"* caveat attached. **Also corrected on the live site:** SaafSaans' artefact card read 628 test functions / 32 files / 161 commits at `5e5037a`; at `667397a` it is **771 / 37 / 236**. Those figures had gone stale in three days, and the card's own source line now says so | 08-11 |
| D64 | **The PII gate blocked the capture commit on a vendored skill's schema.org example; narrowed rather than suppressed, and proved in both directions** | `.agents/skills/seo/SKILL.md:263` ships a JSON-LD `ContactPoint` example whose `telephone` value sits in the range NANP reserves for fiction, beside `https://example.com` and `linkedin.com/company/example` — a documentation placeholder arriving with `addyosmani/web-quality-skills`. **The gate was right to fire and the rule was not touched.** `ALLOW` gained one entry requiring **both** the `"telephone":` JSON-LD key **and** that reserved prefix. Proved in both directions per the loosening rule, not assumed: (1) the vendored example now passes, 103 files clean; (2) `--self-test` still reports *"the gate bites and does not cry wolf"*; (3) a planted **real-looking** number inside a `telephone` field is still caught; (4) a planted **reserved-range** number outside such a field is still caught. **Two unplanned proofs that it bites, both self-inflicted:** it went red on `tests/no-pii.mjs` itself when the justifying comment first spelled a realistic number out, and red again on **this very ledger row** when it quoted the two probe values. Both were reworded to describe the shape rather than print it. A check that catches its own documentation, twice, is a check reading the bytes rather than a rule nodding along | 08-11 |

It now reads `● LIVE · 5:00 PM · AQI 270 · POOR` with **no sample label at all** — and `EXAMPLE PERSONA` turns out not to be a disclaimer but an invitation (*"Fill in your own details and the advice becomes yours"*). It is also unverifiable: there is no studio, there is one engineer shipping systems, and the site's own rule is to state what is proven. The replacement defines the name, states independence, carries no unverifiable claim, and removes the availability ambiguity. **The owner's second and third sentences are kept verbatim** — *"Independent projects are built outside any employer. Permitting screenshots on project pages needed `DESIGN.md`'s "no photographic texture" ban relaxed for that surface. **CLOSED the same day by D66** — `DESIGN.md:345` carries the carve-out. The site is dark-only today; B would be light-only. | D45 | **The third render is what makes rendered-contrast measurement honest** | Building DEF-46's fix, the two-render version (as-is, glyphs removed) looked complete and was wrong in the one case the gate exists for: **when text is nearly the colour of its backdrop, the two renders barely differ, so low contrast is indistinguishable from NO TEXT.** Measured — a deliberately awful `#c49a45` on `#c99b3f` hover reported "no text found" and scored **green**. A third render forces the glyphs to a known probe colour, which locates them independently of their real colour and recovers per-pixel coverage `a = (M − B) / (probe − B)`. Recorded because the two-render version is the obvious design and it fails silently in the direction of passing | 08-09 |

The owner caught it. `tests/no-pii.mjs` now blocks Indian mobile, international, Aadhaar and PAN shapes across `src`, `public`, `docs`, `dist`, `tests`, `assets`. **Proven to bite:** planting the real number in `Layout.astro` makes `git commit` exit 1 and leaves HEAD unchanged. `--self-test` proves both directions — 2/2 planted numbers caught, 0 false positives on `IST (UTC+5:30)`, commit shas and counts. Its own fixtures are assembled from digits at runtime, so **no file in the repo contains a phone-shaped string** and the scanner needs no self-exclusion — the first two attempts failed on themselves, which is how that was found | 08-09 |
| D38 | **The phone number never goes on the public `/cv` page. Email and LinkedIn only** | Verified 09 Aug: the number is **not currently public** — not in `imrohitagrawal/README.md`, not tracked in this repo, not in the built site, which exposes exactly three routes (email, LinkedIn, GitHub). Publishing it would be a **new and irreversible** exposure: an indexed 10-digit number is scraped within days, and Indian mobiles are heavily targeted. It also buys almost nothing — nobody cold-calls a principal engineer off a website; a recruiter emails first and the number comes out in the reply, when he chooses. **Three "clever" fixes are banned as theatre:** reveal-on-click JS (scrapers execute JS), the number as an image (OCR reads it, screen readers cannot), and `@media print` (the number stays in the HTML source, so every scraper gets it and every human does not). Omitting it is the normal shape of a published CV and touches no honesty rule — he is choosing a channel, not misstating a fact | 08-09 |
| D36 | **`Rohit_V3` is the authoritative CV. It wins every conflict with `FINAL-Rohit_Master_Resume`** | Owner's instruction, 09 Aug, recorded verbatim in `assets/inbox/README.md`. The `.md` file is the convenient one to parse and convenience is not authority; it fills gaps only. Conflicts get both values recorded here rather than silently resolved | 08-09 |

### D12 — the install list, not yet executed

```
mattpocock/skills       domain-modeling, grilling, grill-with-docs        ← highest value
project-doc-skills      architecture-and-decisions  (from dist/*.skill)  ← aimed at our problem
project-doc-skills      doc-critic
obra/superpowers        verification-before-completion, test-driven-development,
                        systematic-debugging, requesting-code-review,
                        receiving-code-review, dispatching-parallel-agents
addyosmani/agent-skills code-review-and-quality, performance-optimization
addyosmani/web-quality  performance
ui-ux-pro-max repo      design-review agent (adapted, MIT) — the independent UI lens
```

Three traps:

1. **Do not install both TDD skills.** obra's and addyosmani's share a directory name; the
   second overwrites the first. obra wins on mandatory watch-it-fail.
2. **Install `project-doc-skills` from `dist/*.skill`, never by copying `skills/<name>/`.**
   The source directories are build *inputs* — `house-style.md`, `project-profile.md`, and
   `verify.py` are copied in by `build-skills.sh`. Copying the source yields dangling refs.
3. **`grill-with-docs` is a 245-byte pointer.** Its whole body is *"Run a `/grilling` session,
   using the `/domain-modeling` skill."* Install all three or it points at nothing. Also: the
   "docs" it makes are ADRs and a glossary — it does **not** fetch documentation.

### Conditional — install when the trigger fires

| Skill | Install when |
|---|---|
| `graphify` | Pointed at `docs/` and prose, never `src/` — there is no `.astro` tree-sitter grammar, so the free AST path barely applies and everything falls to the token-costing path |
| `claude-obsidian` | Only if memory living **outside** the site repo is acceptable. Its rule: *"The checkout contains the product. It is not your knowledge vault."* |
| `project-faq` | If a Q&A page is wanted. It emits generated tabbed HTML, a foreign artifact next to Astro |
| `learning-track` | If the writing section becomes a teaching series. It prescribes an 8-critic loop — heavy for one person |
| `onboarding-companion` | If someone else contributes. Doubles as a re-entry doc after a long gap |
| `operations-runbook` | If a case study covers a running service |

### Uncovered capability

**Grounding answers in fetched documentation** has no public skill I could find. `grill-with-docs`
looked like it and is not. Worth watching, since the NarraTwin avatar will need exactly this.

### Skill currency — refresh log

- **2026-08-14** — `npx skills update`: 50 skills checked, 6 updated upstream (brainstorming,
  code-review-and-quality, domain-modeling, performance-optimization, requesting-code-review,
  writing-plans — 8 files; `skills-lock.json` hashes moved with them), and the CLI collapsed
  20 duplicated `.claude/skills/` copies into symlinks to the canonical `.agents/skills/`
  tree. Carry-forward audit ran before commit: all 25 deleted files byte-identical to their
  tracked targets, every symlink resolves. Updated skill bodies activate at the next session
  start (the AGENTS.md restart rule); stated tradeoff — the footer-row package proceeds this
  session because it consumes none of the updated skills.
- **2026-08-24** — `npx skills update` run during the DEF-54 planning phase, before any
  implementation, per the AGENTS.md currency rule. The CLI printed "Updated 51 skill(s)", which
  is its per-fetch message and not evidence: `skills-lock.json` was **byte-identical before and
  after the run**. Three `computedHash` values differ from `main` (`domain-modeling`,
  `grill-with-docs`, `grilling`, all `mattpocock/skills`), but they were fetched in an EARLIER
  session and were already on disk when this session loaded, so they were active throughout and
  no restart was owed. That drift plus 64 modified files under `.agents/skills/` is deliberately
  NOT committed here — it is unrelated to this package and is the owner's to land or discard.
- **2026-08-24, later the same day** — that drift is now LANDED, on the owner's instruction, after
  a carry-forward audit rather than a blind `git add`. What it actually is: **`impeccable`
  4.0.4 -> 4.1.1**, applied consistently to BOTH copies of the skill, plus three
  `mattpocock/skills` hash moves (`domain-modeling`, `grill-with-docs`, `grilling`). 127 tracked
  files, 2 deletions (`scripts/live/ui-core.mjs`), 4 additions
  (`scripts/lib/open-system-browser.mjs`, `scripts/live/ui-surfaces.mjs`, one of each per tree).
  **Landed rather than discarded because the disk state is what this and prior sessions actually
  loaded** — skills load at session start, so discarding would have rolled the next session back
  to 4.0.4 and made it differ from every session that produced the current work. Committing
  records what ran; discarding would have changed it.
- **`impeccable` is an UNLOCKED skill and is now recorded as one**, per AGENTS.md's rule for
  anything installed outside the `skills` CLI. Verified: `skills-lock.json` holds 51 skills and
  `impeccable` is not among them, so it carries no `computedHash` and CANNOT be currency-checked
  the way the other 51 can. Its only version signal is the `version:` field in its own
  `SKILL.md`, which is why the bump above is quoted by number. Re-fetch it deliberately.

The re-measurement is `tests/preload-ab.mjs` — 7 runs per arm, both viewports, fresh context per run, LCP read from `PerformanceObserver`, arms differing by exactly one injected `<link rel=preload>` so nothing is rebuilt between them. **Run against production** (`https://stackclimb.com`), because a localhost run was measured first and rejected as meaningless: LCP there is 40–56 ms with no network latency, which is precisely the condition where a preload has nothing to save. Production result — desktop **A 348 ms vs B 364 ms (+16)**, mobile **A 368 ms vs B 348 ms (−20)**. **The two deltas point in opposite directions and both are dwarfed by run spread (204 ms and 140 ms).** That is the signature of noise, not effect. **This does not reproduce DEF-3's finding** (+44 ms desktop, +96 ms mobile) — and it need not: DEF-3 measured Astro 5, a different hero and a different LCP element. Today's build is simply indifferent to the preload. **Verdict: not worth adding.** Re-opening needs a measurement showing a real gain, not an argument | — |

## Defects found and not yet fixed

| ID | Severity | What | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| DEF-64 | **LOW** | **`DESIGN.md` mandates a component that was removed long ago, in four places.** Surfaced by D118, not caused by it. `global.css:127` states plainly that the swatch tags are gone and `Plate.astro:25` records what replaced the Plate Nº label, yet `DESIGN.md:178` still makes the Three-Color Livery Rule *print* the three colours "as its swatch tag", `:223` describes the plate tag as "Plate Nº + swatch list", `:230` says swatches hide at the 900px breakpoint, and `:344` tells the reader to give every new plate a swatch tag. A cross-model reviewer flagged the inconsistency when D118 deleted the `swatch` and `plate-no` entries from the type scale: the numbers went, the instructions stayed. **Consequence:** a future agent reading the Named Rules section will build a component that does not exist and cannot be styled, because no CSS rule matches it. **Not fixed in D118** — AGENTS.md's rule is not to repair unrelated drift inside another package, and superseding four prose blocks in the design system is a documentation decision, not a type-floor change. The scale comment at `DESIGN.md:24` carries a pointer to this row so the contradiction is not silent | `DESIGN.md:178,223,230,344` · `src/styles/global.css:127` · `src/components/Plate.astro:25` · D118 |
| DEF-63 | **MEDIUM** | **The two labels drawn inside the private figure are the smallest text on the site, and no font-size value in any stylesheet can reach them.** `src/components/figures/PrivateFigure.astro:25,45` set `font-size="9.5"` and `"9"` on inline SVG `<text>`. Those are USER UNITS in a `viewBox="0 0 420 340"`, so what a reader meets is `declared x (renderedWidth / 420)`. Measured with `getScreenCTM()` across the width sweep: **6.26 / 6.60px at 390**, 8.11 / 8.56 at 1024, 13.62 / 14.37 at 768, **10.70 / 11.29 at 1440**, 10.56 / 11.15 at 1920. 1024 is the worst DESKTOP case and neither width the type-floor gate samples sees it. Two consequences worth separating. **First, the readability one:** 6.26px on a phone is smaller than anything the CSS has ever declared, and D118 raised every stylesheet value to 11px without touching these. **Second, an actual visual defect visible on the live site today:** `AEGIS-CONTRACTS` measures ~102.5 user units inside a 96-unit `<rect>`, so the text overflows its own name tag at every width — confirmed by looking at the render, not inferred. **Why it is not fixed here:** clearing 11px at every width needs about 12.2 user units against tags 72 and 96 units wide, which is a redraw of the illustration, not a type change. Two options, both recorded so neither is re-proposed blind: (a) enlarge the tags and the type, (b) DELETE the two `<text>` elements — both names already render as real HTML text on the same plate at 22-32px (`.private-card h3`), the figure is `role="img"` so assistive tech reads its `<title>` and never that text, and blank tags still read as name tags. (b) is smaller and removes duplication, but it deletes visible content from an illustration and is the owner's call, not a presentation detail delegated under P-18. **`tests/type-floor.spec.js` exempts SVG by NAMESPACE, not by an element list** — DEF-10 and DEF-44 are both on this repo's record for a hand-typed list quietly narrowing a gate — and measures the rendered size through the CTM anyway, so the numbers above are reported by the gate rather than lost | `src/components/figures/PrivateFigure.astro:25,45` · `tests/type-floor.spec.js` SVG note · D118 |

The sanctioned dispatch was re-run (`32763604696`) and returned a 42-leg artifact byte-identical to the committed file. **What it does NOT close, stated rather than left to be found:** `--ui` with "Update snapshots -> All" (the UI sends the mode as a CLI override after the config has run), a second Playwright config, and a test writing straight to `testInfo.snapshotPath()`. All four are deliberate acts, not the documented command misfiring; closing them needs a commit-time check, which is in the rejected table with its reason. *Was:* **On Linux, the documented "local convenience" regeneration command overwrites the committed authority, and only prose stops it.** `tests/geometry-baseline.linux.json` is tracked; every other platform's file is gitignored. A developer on Linux running the documented `UPDATE_GEOMETRY=1 npx playwright test tests/geometry.spec.js --workers=1` writes the committed file, and nothing mechanical stops them committing it. The spec header says never hand-generate it, which is prose where this repo's own rule asks for a gate. Found by a round-two reviewer. **Not new and not worse than what exists:** the PNG baselines have the identical asymmetry — `*-darwin.png` is ignored, the `-linux.png` files are tracked, and only the file header stops a Linux laptop overwriting them. Fixing it properly means one gate covering both, which is its own change | `tests/lib/geometry-baseline-io.mjs` · `.gitignore` · D115 |
| DEF-60 | **LOW** | **A structural row converted to `display: block` leaves the geometry gate's population silently, one regeneration later.** The row predicate is a computed `display` of `flex` or `grid` with two or more link/button children, so a row restyled to `block` stops matching. The first run after such a change IS red — all eight `row.*` keys report `in the baseline but NOT measured` (verified by a round-two reviewer, who drove exactly this against `.contact .ctas`). But regenerating the baseline then removes those keys, and the row is outside the gate with an eight-line deletion in the diff as the only signal. This is inherent to any predicate-derived population, and the defence is the one the whole design rests on: the baseline is text, so reviewing the diff is possible. | DEF-56 | **LOW** | **The demoted pixel gate still fails HARD on a one-pixel SIZE difference, and raising the ratio does nothing about it.** Found while shipping D115 and recorded rather than bundled in. `maxDiffPixelRatio` is structurally powerless here: Playwright rejects a size mismatch before any pixel comparison runs, so the tolerance never executes. A second, older limit found on the way and worth stating: `toHaveScreenshot` on a LOCATOR captures that element's own bounding box, so a plate that merely MOVES produces a pixel-identical image. The gate's own header has always claimed it catches "a plate's box moves", and it never could. Both are now geometry's job, so the consequence is CI noise rather than a coverage hole. **Fix, when it is scheduled:** capture a fixed-size viewport clip instead of the element box, which makes a size mismatch impossible and would let the file see a plate move. | ~~DEF-54~~ | **MEDIUM** | **FIXED 08-24 (D115) — the blocking role moved to `tests/geometry.spec.js`; the record below is kept because the measurements in it are why.** **The visual gate is structurally blind to element-scale changes at desktop width — it is not a threshold set too high, and no threshold can fix it.** Found while shipping D112: CI passed `plate-contact-1440` on a commit that adds a whole new button to that plate, while correctly failing the same plate at 390 and 768. Measured, not inferred — the pre-change commit (`df551e9`) was built in a clean worktree, both versions rendered at all three widths, and pixels compared directly: **1440 = 0.82% changed (10,626 of 1,297,440 px) — PASSED; 768 = 21.48% — failed; 390 = 22.19% — failed.** `visual-baselines.spec.js:40` sets `MAX_DIFF_PIXEL_RATIO = 0.03`, chosen in D111 because separate headless-Chromium renders jitter up to 0.02 and real content diffs then measured 0.31-0.56. That reasoning held for a hero-height change and does not generalise: a 149x51 button plus three shifted neighbours is 0.82% of a 1440-wide plate, which is **below the renderer's own 2% noise floor**. Catching it would need a gate near 0.008, under the noise, and every plate would fail every run. The ratio is a fraction of WHOLE-PLATE area, so the larger the plate the smaller any real element change looks. **Consequence, live in the repo:** a regenerated `plate-contact-1440-*-linux.png` is committed in D112 because the old one was wrong, but CI would have gone on passing the stale file indefinitely. Any future element-scale change at desktop is equally invisible. **Third facet, and it explains D111's unsolved mystery — the gate is simultaneously too INSENSITIVE and too SENSITIVE.** Found on DEF-55 (2026-08-24): CI failed with `Expected an image 390px by 1211px, received 390px by 1210px` on `#overview`, a plate that change never touched. A **one-pixel** height difference fails HARD, and `maxDiffPixelRatio` is structurally powerless against it — Playwright rejects a size mismatch before any ratio comparison runs, so the tolerance mechanism never even executes. **Fourteen** committed baselines were stale by exactly 1px (`plate-citevyn/contact/overview/private/saafsaans` across 390 and 768, both projects), drifting in BOTH directions. Because each `plate boundaries at <width>px` test throws at the FIRST mismatch it meets, the gate surfaces them **one per run** — which is exactly why D111 saw three consecutive runs each fail a DIFFERENT plate and read it as unexplained flake. It was never random: it was a queue of stale baselines draining one at a time. Confirmed convergent rather than nondeterministic before acting: regeneration run `32720177963` and PR run `32720616965` produced the SAME heights (both 1210 for `overview-390`), so the committed set was simply stale, not the renderer unstable. All 14 were refreshed in one commit to drain the queue. **This is the strongest argument for the D113 geometry gate:** a 1px height change is invisible to the ratio check yet fatal to the size check, while the same 1px is trivially inside a numeric +/-1px tolerance. **Second consequence, worse than the first: the documented repair path inherited the same blindness.** `gates.yml`'s `update_visual_baselines` step ran `--update-snapshots`, whose preset mode is `changed` (verified: `npx playwright test --help`, Playwright 1.62.1) — so it asks the very comparison that is blind whether a file needs rewriting. On the D112 regeneration run `plate-contact-1440-desktop-linux.png` came back **byte-identical (same md5)** while the plate had gained a whole button, and 56 of 60 files were pixel-identical re-encodings. A baseline that is wrong but inside the tolerance could never be refreshed by the documented process. **Fixed in D112 by switching that step to `--update-snapshots=all`**, so regeneration actually regenerates; the pixel-level triage of which files to commit still applies. **The underlying gate blindness is NOT fixed here, deliberately** — a fix belongs in its own change and is not a new number: scope the assertion to the changed region, or assert on the element's own box rather than the whole plate. Deferred rather than patched because D111 already records three rounds of whack-a-mole on this same file | CI run `32707441086` (4 failed, all `plate-contact` at 390/768; 1440 passed). Ratios measured locally via raw RGB comparison of `git worktree` builds at `df551e9` vs `7649f1d`. `tests/visual-baselines.spec.js:40` |
| DEF-52 | **LOW** — *filed HIGH on 08-12 and downgraded the same day; see the correction row below* | **Cloudflare's Email Obfuscation scrambles every email link, and its own script unscrambles it in the browser. With JavaScript on — which is effectively every visitor — the site works correctly. It degrades only for a no-JS visitor.** Cloudflare's Email Obfuscation is on for the zone. It rewrites all five `mailto:` links into `/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#<hex>` and injects a decoder script. **Measured in a real browser against production, both ways** (`javaScriptEnabled: false`), because the raw HTML alone had already led me to overstate this once: **JS ON** — all four links resolve to `mailto:rohit.ra.agrawal@gmail.com` and `/cv` prints the address. **JS OFF** — all four resolve to `/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#…` and `/cv` prints the literal `[email protected]`. So the defect is real and the degradation is genuine, but it reaches **well under 1% of visitors**, and the owner demonstrated the working case from his own browser. **Two reasons it is still worth turning off, both judgment calls rather than fixes:** the build ships **zero `.js` files** and 1,494 bytes of inline script, and Cloudflare adds a **1,239-byte external script** — nearly doubling the site's JavaScript purely to undo something it did itself, on a site whose argument is that it barely uses any. And it protects nothing: the repo is **public** and the address is in plain text in `PRODUCT.md`, `src/data/cv.js` and `src/layouts/Layout.astro`, so a scraper reads it from GitHub in one request | `curl -s https://stackclimb.com/ \| grep -c 'mailto:'` → **0** (the deployed `dist/index.html` has them). `curl -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}' https://stackclimb.com/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection` → **404**. `grep -c __cf_email__` on live `/cv` → **1**. **No gate can see it:** `playwright.config.js:12` sets `baseURL: http://localhost:4321`, so every contact assertion tests the local build; `post-deploy.mjs` checks build identity and asset reachability, not link reachability. **Owner's call, not a required fix.** Cloudflare dashboard → Scrape Shield → Email Address Obfuscation → off; not a code change — `wrangler`'s OAuth token has no zone scope (`api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/zones` → **403**). YAML validated (`python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(...)"`). Not re-tested against a real missing-secret run (would require pulling the live secret) — the logic is a direct, minimal `if:`/`exit 1` addition, not a rewrite |

Quick supply-chain check on the bump's new transitive deps (`am-i-vibing`, `process-ancestry`, and everything else newly added to the lockfile): only `esbuild`, `fsevents`, `sharp` carry install scripts — all expected native-build packages, nothing new; no network calls or unexpected file writes found in `am-i-vibing`/`process-ancestry` source, both only read `process.env`/`ps` output. **Structural bug found and fixed: `astro preview` silently daemonizes under an AI agent.** Astro 7.2's new `am-i-vibing` dependency (`node_modules/astro/dist/cli/agent.js`) detects agent env vars (`CLAUDECODE`, `CURSOR_TRACE_ID`, `REPL_ID`, etc.) and `astro preview` (`node_modules/astro/dist/cli/preview/index.js:40-58`) responds by backgrounding itself and returning immediately — reproduced: with `CLAUDECODE=1`, `npx astro preview` exits in 738ms while a detached process keeps the port bound (verified via `ps`/`lsof`). That breaks Playwright's `webServer`, which needs the launched command to stay in the foreground; it does not affect real GitHub Actions CI, which sets none of these vars. Fix: `astro preview`'s own opt-out is the env var `ASTRO_PREVIEW_BACKGROUND` (same file, line 40 — `!process.env.ASTRO_PREVIEW_BACKGROUND && isRunByAgent()`); wired into `playwright.config.js`'s `webServer.env` so every future test run gets it automatically, without touching `npm run dev`/`preview`. **Tested both ways, not assumed:** full gate suite (`npm ci`, `npm run build`, `no-pii.mjs` + self-test, `file-budget.mjs` + self-test, `npx playwright test`, `boundary-check.mjs`, `nav-contrast.mjs`) run twice — once with `CLAUDECODE=1` ambient (this session's real shell), once with every Claude/agent env var explicitly stripped (`env -u CLAUDECODE -u AI_AGENT …`, simulating a human shell) — identical results both times: 118 Playwright specs passed, 2 skipped (coarse-pointer, by design), boundary-check worst seam 7.56:1, nav-contrast worst 6.26:1. No leftover daemonized `astro preview` process after either run (`ps`/`lsof` clean) | `npm audit --json` before/after; `ps`/`lsof` timing proof of the daemonize bug; two full gate-suite runs (agent env present / stripped); `playwright.config.js` diff; branch `pr5-astro-major-bump` merged with current `main` |

| ~~DEF-46~~ | — | **FIXED by changing the approach, not by a fifth patch.** The gate measured the resting state only, so a low-contrast `:hover` or `:focus-visible` colour passed it. Four geometry-based fixes each produced a new false positive — sampling above the box read the bar behind the ochre chip (1.16:1 for a ~6:1 pair); bounding corners read the focus ring, because the chip is a 999px pill; a fixed outside gap landed on the ring again at `outline-offset: 3px`; compositing the element's own background reported none while the same probe read it as ochre. **All four shared one false assumption: that the backdrop can be located by geometry.** Replaced with `tests/lib/rendered-contrast.mjs`, which renders the bar **three times** — as-is, with the glyph fill transparent, and with the glyph fill set to a probe colour — and reads the answer off the pixels. B is the exact backdrop under every glyph; M recovers per-pixel coverage so anti-aliasing is handled and the foreground is read from the core of a stroke. Geometry never enters it, so pills, outlines, gradients, blend modes and opacity are all correct by construction. `-webkit-text-fill-color`, not `color`, because the chip's border is `currentColor` and would otherwise vanish and be measured as text | Five mutations, all red: nav ground removed 22/28 positions at 1.02:1 · `opacity: 0.2` 60/96 at 1.35:1 · low-contrast `:hover` 1.02:1 · low-contrast `:focus-visible` 1.22:1 · links hidden → denominator failure |

Added `tests/lib/extract-text.mjs` (dispatches `.pdf` to `pdf-parse`, `.docx` to `mammoth` plus a direct read of header/footer/footnote/endnote XML, everything else to `readFileSync(utf8)`) and wired `tests/no-pii.mjs`'s `scan()` to call it. **What round 1 got wrong, and the fix:** (1) `gates.yml`'s "secrets and personal data" job never ran `npm ci`, so once this file started importing `pdf-parse`/`mammoth` the job crashed with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` before scanning anything — the PII gate was permanently red. (2) The self-test's mutation proof didn't bite: its PDF/DOCX fixtures were uncompressed, so reverting the extraction to raw `buffer.toString('utf8')` still "worked" because the planted digits were visible as raw bytes. Fixed by also reading `word/header*.xml`, `word/footer*.xml`, `word/footnotes.xml`, `word/endnotes.xml` directly from the zip and stripping tags.
