(6) `mammoth`/`pdf-parse`/`jszip` were added with caret ranges; pinned to exact versions per the DEF-17 convention | `node tests/no-pii.mjs --self-test` → `4/4 planted numbers caught`, plus 8 new checks (header, footer, image-flag ×4, uppercase-ext ×2), all `ok`. Mutation proof re-verified after the compression fix: stubbing `.pdf`/`.docx` to raw passthrough now correctly fails 5 of the 8 new checks and drops to `2/4` (previously it silently passed at `4/4`). Real-file proof, both directions: `resume.PDF` (uppercase, renamed from `Rohit_V3.pdf`) is now caught — phone number reported, where before the fix it scanned 0 hits. **`assets/inbox/resume/Rohit_V3.docx` and `FINAL-Rohit_Master_Resume.docx` — the two actual files DEF-37 was about — are now both caught** (`embedded-image` hit, exit 1), where round 1 reported them clean (their phone number is in `word/media/image1.png`, not in any XML part, confirmed by unzip + grep). CI: `npm ci` added to the PII job, verified locally by extracting a clean `git archive HEAD` checkout and confirming `node tests/no-pii.mjs --self-test` now finds `node_modules` present. **Round 3 (08-10) — two MEDIUM findings from round 2 that had blocked auto-merge, both fixed, same PR:** (7) **Fail-open on extraction errors.** `scan()`'s `catch` block on a thrown `extractText()` used to `console.error` and `continue`, so a corrupted or password-encrypted `.pdf`/`.docx` scanned as clean and the gate exited 0 — the one file that most needs a human's eyes was the one the gate said nothing about, and it contradicted the fail-closed rule this same commit had just applied to embedded images. Fixed: a throw on `.pdf`/`.docx` now pushes an `extraction-failed` hit and fails the gate closed, exactly like `embedded-image`. `scan` is now exported from `tests/no-pii.mjs` so the self-test can exercise it end-to-end against real (throwaway, uncommitted) temp files. (8) **`hasImages` blind spot on compressed PDF object streams.** The image detector was a raw-byte regex for `/Subtype /Image` over the whole file; a PDF 1.5+ document can put an image XObject dictionary inside a compressed object stream (`/ObjStm`), invisible to that regex — `hasImages` would silently read `false` with an image actually present. Chose the conservative fix over decompressing untrusted PDF internals: `extract-text.mjs` now also flags `cannotVerify` when the raw bytes contain an `/ObjStm` or `/Type /XRef` marker, and `scan()` treats `cannotVerify` the same as `hasImages` — a `compressed-object-stream` hit, fails the gate. `tests/lib/self-test-fixtures.mjs` grew past the 250-line module budget once these checks were added, so its PDF/DOCX byte-level builders were split out to the new `tests/lib/pdf-docx-fixtures.mjs` (150 lines) | Round 3 self-test: `node tests/no-pii.mjs --self-test` → still `4/4` planted numbers, now **15 checks total, all `ok`** (8 from round 1/2, plus 7 new: ObjStm-has-no-raw-image-signature, ObjStm-flagged-cannotVerify, plain-PDF-not-flagged, valid-files-still-pass-via-scan, corrupt-PDF-fails-closed-via-scan, corrupt-DOCX-fails-closed-via-scan, ObjStm-PDF-fails-closed-via-scan). **Mutation proof, fix (7):** reverted `scan()`'s catch block to the old `continue`-on-error behaviour — `corrupted PDF fails the gate closed` and `corrupted DOCX fails the gate closed` both flipped to `FAIL`, `SELF-TEST FAIL`; reverted back, `SELF-TEST PASS` restored. **Mutation proof, fix (8):** stubbed `cannotVerify` to always return `false` in `extract-text.mjs` — `ObjStm PDF flagged cannotVerify` and `ObjStm PDF fails the gate closed` both flipped to `FAIL`; reverted back, `SELF-TEST PASS` restored. Both mutations run against a real (uncompressed marker) `/ObjStm` PDF fixture and real truncated PDF/DOCX buffers, not synthetic assertions — proving each fix bites and the 18-check standard stayed intact throughout, not weakened to make it pass. **Round 4 (08-10) — one HIGH finding from round 3 review, fixed, same PR (#1):** (9) **Fail-closed only covered exceptions, not silent degradation.** Every prior round's "fail closed" logic (extraction-failed, embedded-image, compressed-object-stream) triggers only when `pdf-parse`/`mammoth` either throw or leave a raw-byte marker behind. Measured directly against `pdf-parse@2.4.5`: flipping a handful of interior bytes of an otherwise-valid `/FlateDecode` content stream (same length, so the `/Length` dictionary entry still matches) makes `pdf-parse` return `""` per page and report success — no throw, no marker, nothing to catch. Measured against `mammoth`: a technically well-formed, empty `<w:body></w:body>` makes `extractRawText()` return `""` with zero warning messages, also no throw. Either case previously produced `hasImages:false, cannotVerify:false`, an empty/near-empty `text`, and the file reported clean with zero PII hits — the exact fail-open pattern rounds 1–3 were supposed to close, one layer deeper, exactly as round 3's reviewer predicted. Fixed: `extractPdf` now computes meaningful characters per page (stripping `pdf-parse`'s own `"-- N of M --"` page-separator boilerplate first, so that can't pad a truly empty page into looking like content) and flags `cannotVerify` with `reason: 'sparse-text'` when that average falls under 15 chars/page; `extractDocx` does the same over total combined (body + header/footer/footnote/endnote) text, floor 15 chars. Both floors are deliberately low — chosen to bite reliably on total degradation (0 chars, what the libraries actually return per the measurement above) while sitting well under the self-test's own real fixture text (~25–35 chars), so there is no tension between catching degradation and not crying wolf; false positives above the floor are cheap (a human looks), false negatives below it are the thing this gate exists to prevent. `scan()` in `tests/no-pii.mjs` now branches on `reason` to report a `sparse-extraction` hit with its own message, distinct from `compressed-object-stream` | Round 4 self-test: `node tests/no-pii.mjs --self-test` → still `4/4` planted numbers, now **23 checks total, all `ok`** (15 from rounds 1–3, plus 8 new: degraded-stream-PDF-flagged, empty-body-DOCX-flagged, real-content-PDF-not-flagged ×1, real-content-DOCX-not-flagged ×1, plus the same four proved again end-to-end via `scan()` against real temp files). **Mutation proof:** set both `MIN_CHARS_PER_PDF_PAGE` and `MIN_DOCX_CHARS` to `0` (disabling the new check, simulating the pre-fix behaviour) — `degraded-stream PDF flagged cannotVerify`, `empty-body DOCX flagged cannotVerify`, and both corresponding `scan()`-level `sparse-extraction` checks all flipped to `FAIL`, `SELF-TEST FAIL`; restored, `SELF-TEST PASS` returned. The degraded-PDF fixture is a real same-length byte-flip inside a real `/FlateDecode` stream (not a synthetic assertion) and was independently confirmed against the installed `pdf-parse@2.4.5` to parse without throwing and return `""` per page — proving the mutation matches the real library's actual failure mode, not an assumption about it. Full gate suite re-run clean after the fix: build, both self-tests, both live scans, 64 Playwright specs (2 pre-existing skips, unrelated), boundary-check (worst seam 7.56:1), nav-contrast (worst 6.26:1). **Round 5 (08-10) — two HIGH findings from round 4 review (same underlying gap, reported twice), fixed, same PR (#1):** (10) **Round 4's sparse-text check was a WHOLE-DOCUMENT average, so one degraded page/part hid behind a healthy rest-of-document.** PDF: `meaningfulChars.length / pageCount` against the 15-char floor — a 2-page PDF with page 1 intact (dense real text) and page 2 silently degraded to `""` averages above the floor because page 1 alone clears it; `cannotVerify` stayed `false` and a phone number planted on page 2 was never recovered, never flagged, 0 hits. DOCX: body + every header/footer/footnote/endnote part were summed into one pooled count against the same floor — a healthy body masked a degraded footer the same way. Measured directly, not assumed: built a real 2-page PDF (page 2's `/FlateDecode` stream byte-flipped, same technique as round 4's `degradeStream`) and a real DOCX (footer XML reduced to an empty `<w:p></w:p>`), ran both through the actual `extractText()`/`scan()`, confirmed `cannotVerify:false` and 0 hits before the fix. Fixed: PDF now checks each page's own character count against the floor, splitting on `pdf-parse`'s own `"-- N of M --"` page separators (falls back to the old whole-document average only if the split doesn't yield the expected number of chunks); DOCX now checks the body and each present header/footer/footnote/endnote part against the floor independently. `cannotVerify` fires if ANY single page or part falls under 15 chars, not just the average. New fixture builders (`buildMultiPagePdf` in `pdf-docx-fixtures.mjs`; `emptyFooter` option on `buildDocx`) construct genuine multi-page/multi-part documents with independent per-page/per-part content, which the pre-round-5 fixture builders (single content stream only) could not do — the gap was invisible to the self-test by construction, exactly the same class of miss as the DEF-37-round-1 lesson two rows below. `tests/lib/self-test-fixtures.mjs` split its round-5 checks into the new `tests/lib/self-test-sparse-density.mjs` to stay under the 250-line module budget. **Round 6 (08-10) — owner's explicit decision: replace enumeration with structure, after 5 rounds of hole-by-hole patching each closed one place and the next round found a different one (PDF off-canvas text, PDF `/Annot` FreeText contents, DOCX `comments.xml`, DOCX footnote split-runs, DOCX drawingML text box, all reproduced with real constructed fixtures by round 5's reviewer, 0/5 caught).** Three structural changes, all three required: (11) **DOCX: stopped using a named-part allowlist.** `extractDocx` now also opens the `.docx` zip and reads EVERY `*.xml` entry inside it — `word/`, `docProps/`, anywhere — strips tags, and appends the result as a backstop, on top of (not instead of) mammoth's body extraction and the existing named header/footer/footnote/endnote reads. New `tests/lib/docx-archive-scan.mjs` (`scanArchiveXmlParts`), reusing the existing `stripXmlTags`. This is what catches `comments.xml` (never a named part) and a drawingML text box (`w:txbxContent`) living inside `document.xml` itself, which mammoth's `extractRawText` does not walk into. (12) **PDF: added a raw-decoded-stream pass, independent of page geometry.** `pdf-parse`'s `getText()` is rendered, visible-page text — measured directly: a `Tj` operator pushed off the `/MediaBox` via a large `Td` offset, in the SAME content stream as text `pdf-parse` does extract, is silently dropped, no error. New `tests/lib/pdf-raw-scan.mjs` (`extractPdfRawText`) decompresses every `/FlateDecode` stream found anywhere in the raw PDF bytes (this also extends the existing `/ObjStm` detection from flag-only to actually decoding it, since an ObjStm's dictionary is FlateDecode too) and scans the decoded bytes directly; it also extracts literal, uncompressed strings next to a `/Contents` key, since `/Annot /FreeText /Contents` is commonly stored that way and is never part of any page content stream at all. Runs as an addition to `pdf-parse`'s structured text, not a replacement — the existing per-page sparse-text check (round 5) still runs on the structured extraction only. (13) **Loosened the phone-number regex to survive XML-run splitting, without reopening "any digits near whitespace".** A DOCX footnote's phone number split across separate `<w:r>` runs turns tag-stripping's tag-to-space replacement into a run of several spaces at each seam (one space per stripped tag; a run boundary is at least 2 tags). Added a second rule, `phone-in-split`, alongside the unchanged original `phone-in`: it accepts a gap between digits ONLY if that gap is 2-4 whitespace/separator characters — never a single bare space. Measured directly why the single-space case had to stay excluded: an earlier draft that allowed 0-4 characters matched real SVG coordinate strings in `PrivateFigure.astro`/`QuorumFigure.astro` (`"76 102 54 130"` reads as 10 digits with single-space gaps) as false positives — tightening the floor from 0 to 2 cleared them while still catching the round-6 footnote fixture, whose stripped-tag gap is 4 spaces. New `tests/lib/round6-fixtures.mjs` builds all 5 reproduction fixtures; new `tests/lib/self-test-round6.mjs` runs the checks, wired into `self-test-fixtures.mjs`. Rounds 1-5's fail-closed backstops (embedded image, ObjStm/compressed-xref, extraction-throws, sparse/degraded text) are all still in place, unchanged — round 6 adds coverage on top, it does not remove the net | Round 5 self-test: `node tests/no-pii.mjs --self-test` → still `4/4` planted numbers, now **28 checks total, all `ok`** (23 from rounds 1–4, plus 5 new: 2-page-PDF-page-2-degraded-flagged, 2-page-PDF-both-real-not-flagged, DOCX-degraded-footer-flagged, DOCX-healthy-footer-not-flagged, plus the page-2-degraded case proved again end-to-end via `scan()` against a real temp file). **Written RED first, per TDD:** the new assertions were added before the fix and confirmed to fail exactly on the reviewer's two scenarios (`2-page PDF, only page 2 degraded, still flagged cannotVerify: FAIL`; `DOCX, healthy body but degraded footer, still flagged cannotVerify: FAIL`), then turned green by the per-page/per-part fix, nothing else. **Mutation proof:** reverted the PDF check back to `(meaningfulChars.length / pageCount) < MIN_CHARS_PER_PDF_PAGE` and the DOCX check back to summing body+parts into one pooled `meaningfulChars.length < MIN_DOCX_CHARS` — all 3 round-5 "still flagged" assertions flipped to `FAIL`, `SELF-TEST FAIL`; restored, `SELF-TEST PASS` returned. Full gate suite re-run after this fix, all green (see command block in the handoff). **Round 6 self-test:** `node tests/no-pii.mjs --self-test` → still `4/4` planted numbers, now **38 checks total, all `ok`** (28 from rounds 1–5, plus 10 new: the 5 fixtures each caught via direct `extractText()` + confirmed clean on the paired non-planted fixture, then each of the same 5 proved again end-to-end via `scan()` against a real temp file). **Written RED first, per TDD:** all 5 fixtures confirmed 0/5 caught against the pre-round-6 code (named-part allowlist, structured-only PDF extraction, contiguous-digit regex) before any fix landed. **Mutation proof, all 3 changes independently load-bearing:** (a) commented out the `scanArchiveXmlParts` call in `extractDocx` — `DOCX comments.xml caught` and `DOCX drawingML text box caught` both flipped to `FAIL`, the other 3 round-6 checks stayed `ok`; restored, `SELF-TEST PASS`. (b) commented out the `extractPdfRawText` call in `extractPdf` — `PDF off-canvas Td text caught` and `PDF /Annot FreeText Contents caught` both flipped to `FAIL`, the other 3 stayed `ok`; restored, `SELF-TEST PASS`. (c) reverted `phone-in-split` back to requiring contiguous digits — `DOCX footnote, digits split across runs, caught` flipped to `FAIL` alone, the other 4 stayed `ok`; restored, `SELF-TEST PASS`. **False-positive proof against real production content, not just fixtures:** `node tests/no-pii.mjs` (the live scan, not self-test) run against the actual repo both before and after the regex change — before the `{2,4}`-floor fix, the loosened `phone-in-split` draft matched real SVG coordinate strings in `src/components/figures/PrivateFigure.astro` and `QuorumFigure.astro` and in `dist/index.html` (their built output), 9 false-positive hits; after the fix, `✓ PII gate: 100 files scanned, no personal contact details found` — zero hits, confirmed clean both before this PR's changes and after. Full gate suite re-run clean after round 6: `npm ci`, `npm run build` (no new warnings), both self-tests (`no-pii.mjs`, `file-budget.mjs`), both live scans, 64 Playwright specs (2 pre-existing skips, unrelated to this change), `boundary-check.mjs` (worst seam 7.56:1, AA), `nav-contrast.mjs` (worst 6.26:1). New files stay within the 250-line/32,000-byte/120-char module budget: `tests/lib/docx-archive-scan.mjs` (40 lines), `tests/lib/pdf-raw-scan.mjs` (93 lines), `tests/lib/round6-fixtures.mjs` (148 lines), `tests/lib/self-test-round6.mjs` (104 lines); `tests/lib/pdf-docx-fixtures.mjs` (236) and `tests/lib/self-test-fixtures.mjs` (224) stayed under budget without needing a further split. **Round 7 (08-10) — one MEDIUM finding from PR-1 review, fixed, same PR (#1):** (14) **`phone-in-split`'s {2,4}-width gap alone did not distinguish a real split phone number from ordinary tabular content.** The round-6 fixture (a footnote's digits split across XML runs) and a 3-space-aligned table of single-digit columns (a plain-text table of scores or coordinates that happens to total 10 digits starting 6-9) both fall inside the same 2-4-character gap width — width was never the real signal. What differs is gap COUNT: the round-6 fixture splits into 4 multi-digit groups (`987`, `65`, `432`, `10`) joined by exactly 3 gaps, because Word/XML run boundaries are comparatively rare; a table split digit-by-digit needs a gap between every column — 9 gaps for 10 digits. Fixed: the `phone-in-split` rule gained a `filter` (checked in `scan()`'s match loop, and in both self-test `matches()` helpers) that rejects a match with more than 3 gaps. `tests/lib/self-test-fixtures.mjs` grew a `tableRow` fixture (`'7   1   2   3   4   5   6   7   8   9'`, 9 gaps, would have been a false positive) in its clean-text set | Round 7, written RED first per TDD: `tableRow` confirmed as a false positive against the pre-fix regex (`SELF-TEST FAIL`, false positive count > 0) before the `filter` was added. After the fix: `node tests/no-pii.mjs --self-test` → `SELF-TEST PASS`, all pre-existing checks still `ok` (the round-6 footnote fixture's 3-gap match still caught — filter threshold of ≤3 keeps it). Full gate suite re-run clean: `npm ci`, `npm run build`, both self-tests, `node tests/no-pii.mjs` (100 files, 0 hits), `file-budget.mjs` both modes, 64 Playwright specs (2 pre-existing skips), `boundary-check.mjs` (worst seam 7.56:1, AA), `nav-contrast.mjs` (worst 6.26:1). `tests/no-pii.mjs` (203 lines/9,784 bytes), `tests/lib/self-test-fixtures.mjs` (240 lines/13,134 bytes), `tests/lib/self-test-round6.mjs` (112 lines/6,521 bytes) — all within the 250-line/32,000-byte/120-char module budget. **Round 8 (08-10) — owner's decision: 7 rounds of content-scanning inside PDF/DOCX did not converge, so stop relying on it alone.** The gate now has two layers: (a) an unconditional block — `scan()` fails any tracked `.pdf`/`.docx` outright, without reading it, as a `pdf-docx-unconditional` hit for manual review; this, not the content scanner, is the real guarantee. (b) content scanning from rounds 1-7 still runs as best-effort early signal, with known gaps (hex-encoded PDF annotations, DOCX numeric character references, fixture-tuned thresholds) left open. A clean content scan is not proof of PII-free — only (a) plus a human review is. Zero blast radius today: no `.pdf`/`.docx` is currently tracked | Self-test: valid PDF/DOCX now assert exactly one `pdf-docx-unconditional` hit and no content-rule false positive — `SELF-TEST PASS`, rounds 1-7 checks still `ok`. Mutation both directions: planted a clean throwaway `.pdf`/`.docx` under `public/`, `node tests/no-pii.mjs` failed with `pdf-docx-unconditional` only; removed, gate clean again (104 files scanned) |

**Measured performance baseline — the site is already fast.** Lighthouse 13.4.1: desktop **100**
(LCP 0.4s, CLS 0.007, TBT 0ms), mobile **98** (LCP 2.4s, CLS 0.002, TBT 0ms). Scroll is locked at
60fps with **zero long tasks** on both. Do not "optimise" scroll, animation, or the blend overlay.

## Rejected — do not re-propose without new information

| Option | Why rejected |
|---|---|
| A commit-message trailer marking a baseline as CI-generated | Rejected for DEF-59. Merge styles here are mixed — `2be2b87` and `2db0eb6` carry a `(#NN)` squash signature, `b7ee8e1` and `d2ce47b` do not — and GitHub only PREFILLS the squash body, so a trailer can be edited away at merge time. The push-to-main run would then go red on a commit nobody can un-make, and `deploy` `needs` that job, so the regeneration commit would never reach production. A bigger defect than the one being fixed |
| A hash manifest of the committed baselines, written by CI | Rejected for DEF-59. The sanctioned visual regeneration rewrites all 60 PNGs but only the pixel-changed ones are committed — 56 of 60 came back as re-encodings on the D112 run — so a CI-authored manifest would never match the committed subset and would block the path it exists to protect |
| A `.githooks/pre-commit` refusal on a staged baseline | Rejected for DEF-59. With the write refused at source there is nothing left for it to catch except the legitimate commit-the-artifact step, and its only escape is `--no-verify`, which switches off the staged-PII scan, the inbox check, the `.env` check and gitleaks in the same keystroke. See also DEF-62: the hook binds on one machine anyway |
| Keying the CI exemption on `CI=true` | Rejected for DEF-59. Many local tools and shell profiles export it, and `playwright.config.js` already reads it for something else, so it would hand the exemption out by accident. `GITHUB_ACTIONS` is set whenever GitHub Actions runs a workflow, hosted or self-hosted, and by very little else. It is not authorization — anyone can export it — and the module says so in those words |
| Hardcoding `platform === 'linux'` in the guard | Rejected for DEF-59 by `.gitignore`'s own argument: naming platforms one at a time "left any future one tracked by default, which is the wrong direction for a rule whose whole point is that only the linux file may be committed" |
| Redirecting the snapshot path outside CI with `snapshotPathTemplate` | Rejected for DEF-59. Playwright resolves ONE path for both the read and the write, so redirecting the write redirects the comparison too and a local run would grade itself against a file it had just produced |
| Verifying the regeneration run id through the Actions API | Rejected for now. `gates.yml`'s `permissions:` block grants `contents: read` and `pull-requests: read`, which makes every unnamed scope `none`, so `actions` is `none` today. DEF-39 is the recorded scar from a required gate that needed an API call it did not have permission for. Reconsider only with the permission change made deliberately and its own review |
| WordPress | $300/yr, wrong runtime for authored interactive graphics, and it argues against a reliability engineer's own positioning |
| DigitalOcean App Platform | 1 GiB transfer on the free static tier; $5/month per always-on container against Fly's $0.08 idle |
| Vercel | Hobby tier prohibits commercial use; Pro is $20/user/month |
| Migrating apps off Fly to Cloud Run | Saves pennies, costs a GCP project, Artifact Registry, IAM, and three migrations |
| Replacing the plate world entirely | Would rebuild six authored figures and the leader-line interaction to solve a page-structure problem |
| `phuryn/pm-skills` | 66 of 68 fail the how/where-not/what-not test. `positioning-ideas` would drag competitor-matrix framing into the copy |
| `andrej-karpathy-skills` | Not knowledge management — one 2.5 KB file duplicating the global `CLAUDE.md`. Claims MIT in frontmatter; **no LICENSE file exists** |
| `notebooklm-py` | Drives an unofficial Google API and stores a bearer credential on disk. Wrong shape for maintaining a repo's knowledge |
| `usage-guide` | Grade-2 reading level for end users operating a product. No such reader here |
| `security-and-hardening` | Five of six sections are server-side. This site has no server, no auth, no database |
| `VoltAgent`, `agentskills`, `vercel-labs`, `wednesday-solutions` | Two are not skills at all. The others are React/runtime-bound |
| Hero figure as a node-and-line SVG diagram (gate glyph + 4 checkbox nodes) | D105. Owner's verdict: "pathetic" — generic flowchart/infographic language with no relationship to the ledger and CapStrip already sitting in the same plate. Replaced with "the seal" |
| Hero figure as a stamped "VERIFIED" typographic seal | D105/D106. Also shown, also rejected ("pathetic," again) — owner asked for a real image instead of more typography. Replaced with a fanned composite of the four systems' own real screenshots (D106) |
| Tuning `MAX_DIFF_PIXEL_RATIO` to make the pixel gate catch element-scale changes | DEF-54/D115. Refuted by measurement, not opinion: on an untouched plate render noise was **3.17%** while a plate that gained a whole button changed **2.42%** — the noise EXCEEDS the signal, so no threshold orders them correctly. Raising the ratio to 0.15 as part of DEMOTING that gate is a different move and is what shipped |
| Raising the per-pixel colour threshold instead | DEF-54/D115. Swept >12 to >120; noise stays above signal at every level, because the noise is glyph re-hinting flipping `#f2ebdd` to `#1b2440` — a maximal per-pixel delta, not soft anti-aliasing |
| Scoping the screenshot to the changed row and keeping pixels blocking | DEF-54/D115. It does flip the ordering (11.55% vs 6.45%) but at only ~1.8x margin, and every band on this site contains text. Kept solely as the coarse net |
| Deleting the visual baselines entirely | DEF-54/D115. Loses the catastrophe net and the PNG a human can look at, for no gain |
| Generating the geometry baseline on any machine, because "numbers do not vary by OS" | D115. **Refuted by measurement, and it was the plan's own stated premise.** A linux baseline and a darwin one at the same commit differ on **148 of 828 keys past the slack, worst 42px**; the first CI run of the branch was red on 38 of 42 legs with an untouched tree. macOS and Linux rasterize the same self-hosted fonts to different advance widths, so text wraps elsewhere and blocks are different heights. The baseline is platform-suffixed; only the linux file is committed |
| Having the geometry spec build the project matrix itself (one spec opening its own browser contexts) | D115. It removes the parallel-write race, but re-implements `playwright.config.js`'s project list inside the spec — add a third project and the gate silently stops measuring it. That is the hand-typed-list trap DEF-10, DEF-44 and `touch.css` are already on the record for. Solved with `--workers=1` plus a throw if the flag is missing |
| Writing our own visual-review skill | `design-review` already exists, MIT, and is battle-tested |

---

## Corrections — mistakes made, kept so they are not repeated

| What I claimed | What was true | Lesson |
|---|---|---|
| **"Every email link on production is dead without JavaScript… the site's only contact route now requires it."** Filed as **DEF-50, HIGH**, and named the single next action | **True only for a no-JS visitor, which is well under 1% of people.** The owner replied with a screenshot of `/cv` rendering his address correctly and the button opening his mail client. He was right. Measured properly afterwards, in a real browser both ways: **JS ON** — all four links resolve to `mailto:…` and `/cv` prints the address; **JS OFF** — all four resolve to `/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#…`. The degradation is real; the severity was not. Downgraded to **LOW** and rewritten | **I read the raw HTML and reported a user-facing outcome from it.** The HTML was accurate and the conclusion was not, because the script that repairs it runs after the HTML is written — the same class of error as testing `dist/` and reporting on production. **A claim about what a visitor experiences must come from loading the page as a visitor**, with `javaScriptEnabled` set both ways. And severity is part of the claim: leading with an emergency that only reaches 1% of visitors spends the owner's attention on the wrong thing |
| Filed two new defects as **DEF-50** and **DEF-51** | **`DEF-50` was already taken** — a real, closed defect from 08-09 (a phone number and LinkedIn QR rendered as pixels inside four visiting-card PNGs), and **`D53` depends on it by name**: *"Gated on DEF-50 being closed first."* Reusing the number would have made that sentence point at a Cloudflare setting. Renumbered to **DEF-52** and **DEF-53** | **Read the highest id in the whole file, not the last row of the table.** Struck rows keep their numbers, and `grep -o 'DEF-[0-9]*' \| sort -t- -k2 -n -u \| tail -1` answers it in one command. I appended to the top of the table and assumed the count |
| DEF-37 round 1: "The PII gate can now see inside `.pdf` and `.docx`" | True for `.pdf` (both real files caught). **False for the two real `.docx` files DEF-37 was written about** — `Rohit_V3.docx` and `FINAL-Rohit_Master_Resume.docx` both scanned clean, because their phone number sits inside an embedded image (`word/media/image1.png`), which no text-extraction library can see. The self-test's 4/4-caught result only proved the plumbing worked for a number planted as literal text — it could not have caught this by construction | A self-test built from synthetic fixtures proves the code path, not the real-world case. When the defect names specific real files, run the fix against those files, not only against fixtures shaped like them |
| While verifying DEF-58 I ran `grep '<section...class="plate"...id='` over `dist/cv.html`, got **0**, and was one step from refuting the defect as wrong | **DEF-58 is right.** The element is `<article class="cv plate" id="cv">`. My pattern was scoped to `<section>`, so it could not match an `<article>` no matter what. A real tag-and-class-token parse finds exactly 1 | A regex is not a DOM query. Scoping a search to the tag you EXPECT hides the case that matters — and the near-miss was refuting a correct finding, which is worse than missing one |
| `docs/STATUS.md` D112 cited `.impeccable/critique/2026-08-23T21-34-02Z__src-pages-index-astro.md` as its evidence | The file was **untracked** — it existed on one laptop and nowhere else, so the citation resolved for nobody but the author. The D102 defect class, repeated. Both that snapshot and its uncited sibling from the same run are committed here; `.impeccable/critique/` was never gitignored and three earlier snapshots were already tracked, so this was omission, not policy | A citation is only evidence if the thing cited is where the reader can reach it |
| "CiteVyn uses Arize" | 27 matches were all the word **"summarize"** | A substring is not a token |
| "No file-size rule exists in NarraTwin" | It does — `ADR/0047:55`. I searched feature branches, not `main` | Read `main` |
| "You have no engineering skills" | `SKILL_LOCK.md` locks Addy Osmani, GitHub Spec Kit, and PM Skills | Scope a claim to what was actually checked |
| "Fonts are 76% of the payload" | Fonts are `unicode-range` subset; real cost ~91K. Two PNGs are 60% | Measure what is fetched, not what exists |
| "ui-ux-pro-max has independent heuristics" | 115 of its 208 rules cite the same WCAG/HIG standards impeccable uses | Independently compiled is not independently derived |
| "Going public needs one reworded line" | Four lines across three files; `PRODUCT.md` was worse | Scan before asserting scope |
| The architect's proof that `.js-ground` fails open | The probe did not isolate the variable — at scroll 0 the **healthy** page also shows 14 nodes at 1.03:1, because `#private` was never scrolled into view. Conclusion right, evidence invalid | A right answer from a wrong proof is still a wrong proof |
| Security: "LICENSE and RCA-002 are untracked" | Both present in `git archive HEAD`. Two false claims, each killable by one command | Widest brief, lowest floor — verify every individual claim |
| "`project-doc-skills` has dangling references" | It does not. The earlier review read `skills/<name>/`, a build **input**. The product is `dist/*.skill`, a 66 KB zip with all 16 files | Test the artifact that ships, not the source that builds it |
| "`project-doc-skills` is a poor fit" | `architecture-and-decisions` is now the **second-best** install of everything evaluated | A verdict inherited from one agent is a claim, not a finding |
| SaafSaans "started at Build with AI as a three-hour build" | The repo records no duration anywhere. The first commit of the day is "existing UI baseline", and the case study dates the Streamlit app to before 18 July | An owner-stated figure the design doc itself said to label went to the site unlabelled. The rule was written and then not applied in the same change |
| "The certificate is valid and the address resolves" | The address resolves; the server presents no certificate at all | A sentence with two clauses needs two checks. Half a verified claim is an unverified claim |
| A commit message saying "nothing said twice" | Relocation was stated in two places, in two wordings | Claiming an absence needs a search for it, not a memory of intending it |
| `tests/contact.spec.js` "prevents the contact plate being deleted" | Deleting the entire plate passed. So did hiding the whole page | A gate written after its fix has never been observed failing. Watch it fail or it proves nothing |
| "The buildathon's first slot was 16:45, so the 18 July commits precede the event" | **There is no 16:45 slot.** The event ran 09:00–17:30 IST (owner-confirmed, 2026-08-08). The commits at 14:17, 14:49 and 15:21 are *inside* it. I relayed a reviewer's figure into the brief of a six-agent workflow without checking it, and it reached this ledger twice | An unverified figure put into a shared brief is not one error, it is one per agent. Check before you brief |
| Five review passes read the footer and none read its words | The critique counted the colophon at 123 characters and 10.56px and never asked what it said. "work sample" appears **0 times** across the entire critique. Two review agents and a cross-model pass also missed it | A measurement is not a reading. A reviewer told to check typography checks typography; nobody was asked whether the sentence was worth saying |
| `AGENTS.md`: "Cloudflare Pages auto-deploys every push to `main`. There is no gate between a push and production" — the standing debt's opening sentence, repeated in D14 and D15 | **The project has no git integration at all.** `wrangler pages project list` → `Git Provider: No`. Nothing has ever deployed on push; every deploy is a manual CLI run. The stated risk was the opposite of the real one: there *is* a gate, and the real problem is silent drift — production sat six commits behind `main` and nothing said so | A deployment model recorded in prose and never checked against the platform. One command settles it, and the sentence had been repeated in three places for two days without anyone running it |
| Gates run immediately after a deploy go red and then pass | **Three occurrences on 09 Aug, all transient, none reproducible.** (1) the seam gate reported `1.00:1 at "overview → citevyn"`, clean on the next three runs; (2) `/cv` returned **404** twice while its own content was being served, correct within a minute; (3) the nav gate reported a failure, clean on the immediate re-run at 15.60:1. **Cause UNVERIFIED** — CDN propagation is the leading hypothesis and it is not proven; a half-propagated deploy serving new HTML against old CSS would produce exactly this. **This is not a curiosity, it is a blocker for approach C**: the standing debt's target is deployment through CI with gates, and a post-deploy gate that goes red one run in three is worse than no gate — it trains you to re-run until green, which is how a real failure gets waved through. Any post-deploy check needs a settle wait and an N-of-M rule stated up front, NOT a bare retry | Wait, measure, measure again. And never let a gate's remedy be "run it until it passes" |
| `docs/positioning-phase0.md`: the method was "`brand-positioning`, paired with `grilling`" | **`grilling` was never invoked.** The adversarial pass was done inline and then described as the skill. The owner caught it by asking whether appropriate skills had been used | A claim about *method* is a claim, and it needs the same check as a claim about a number. Writing it into a committed document — on a site whose subject is claims that survive a check — is the worst place to be loose. Say what was run |
| `/cv` was built as "the CV work (D31, approach B)" | **None of the five ResumeSkills was invoked** — `tech-resume-optimizer`, `resume-quantifier`, `executive-resume-writer`, `portfolio-case-study-writer`, `job-description-analyzer`. D35 installed them for exactly this task, one session earlier, after a two-round search. The page was built from first principles instead | Installing a skill is not using it. The register that records WHICH skill a task needs should be checked at the start of that task, not at the end of the session |
| `docs/positioning-phase0.md`: *"Everything else in this file is applied"* | **Two of its four Changed rows were not.** The headline still shipped D23's rejected truncation, and the contact plate never received the owner's sentence — while the five-title problem it claimed to fix had merely relocated there. Found by `/impeccable critique` | **Second** unchecked claim in the same file, after the `grilling` provenance line. Both are a planning document asserting its own execution. A doc that says "applied" needs the same gate as a number that says 52 |
| Oracle's numbers and `paint-grain.png` are the biggest asset problem | `dist/og.png` is **337,549 bytes** — twice `paint-grain.png` at 166,280, and the largest file in the build. I recorded paint-grain as the biggest and never listed the build by size | Rank by measurement, not by the asset you happened to be looking at |
| The workflow judge's S1: the event page forbids pre-built work | Two fetches of that page returned two different schedules, and neither contained the quoted rules. The finding was never reproducible | The layer built to refute the others needs refuting too. A quote is evidence only if a second fetch returns it |
| **"`FINAL-Rohit_Master_Resume.md` CONTAINS A PHONE NUMBER"** — stated in D37, D38, D39, `assets/inbox/README.md` and the handoff | **It does not.** The `.md` contact block carries e-mail, LinkedIn and Visa only, and the whole file contains **zero** digit-runs of eight or more. The number is in **`Rohit_V3.pdf` and `FINAL-Rohit_Master_Resume.pdf`** — the contact line, beside the e-mail. `textutil` renders neither `.docx` to a phone number, so the plain-text extractions are clean too. **The conclusion was right and the location was wrong**, which is the dangerous combination: the ledger pointed the next reader at the one resume file that is safe, and away from the two that are not. It also hid DEF-37 — a `.md` is scanned by the gate, a `.pdf` is not | Naming the wrong file is not a smaller error than missing the risk. Locate PII by running the scanner's rules over every candidate, not by reading the one file that is easiest to open |
| Oracle "MTTD reduced ~35%, release cycle time reduced ~25%" — `PRODUCT.md:160`, inherited by D25, `ACTION-PLAN.md:86` and `positioning-decisions.md:64` | The authoritative CV **never says MTTD.** It says *"reducing root-cause analysis time by 35%"* (time to **diagnose**) and *"reducing release validation time by 25%"* (a **component** of cycle time). Both restatements are larger than their source | A metric renamed is a metric overclaimed. MTTD and RCA-time are different numbers; so are validation time and cycle time. This was a paraphrase that four documents then treated as the fact |
| The handoff: "Do NOT add to src/styles/global.css. It is at 498 of a grandfathered 500" | **It was at 505.** The ceiling was already breached when the warning was written; the warning itself was the evidence nobody had run `wc -l`. The breach is DEF-47 and the ceiling is now a CI gate | A limit stated in prose is a limit nobody has measured lately. Numbers in a handoff need the same check as numbers on the site |
| The ledger's DEF-14 mutation: "an empty link `href=\"/x\"` planted in the nav went undetected" | Planted verbatim it IS detected — by `links.spec.js`, as a 404, before axe ever runs. Red for the wrong reason: the a11y hole was real but the recorded mutation could no longer isolate it once the resolve gate existed. A resolvable `href="/cv"` shows the true hole | A recorded mutation ages as other gates land. Re-run it before trusting it, and record WHAT it fails on, not only THAT it fails |
| DEF-17: "One `npm install` moves the Chromium binary and invalidates every future baseline" | CI runs `npm ci` from a lockfile that pinned all four packages, and zero screenshot baselines exist to invalidate. The caret was a real local-drift risk and is now pinned; the stated blast radius described infrastructure this repo does not have | Sizing a defect means checking what it can actually reach, not what the same defect could reach somewhere else |
| The CV is the source of truth for project numbers | It is stale on CiteVyn: `Rohit_V3` says *"361+ automated tests and a 50/50 golden evaluation suite"*. DEF-31 counted **1,036 test functions across 110 files** and **52** golden cases at `df8cfc3`. **The site is right and the CV is wrong** | D36 makes `Rohit_V3` authoritative over the *other resume*, not over a count run against the repo. Authority is scoped to the conflict it was written to settle |
